# Roadmap views

Generated by `python3 scripts/check-roadmap.py --write` from [release-plan.json](release-plan.json). Do not edit this view. Read [policy and dispositions](roadmap.md) and [first-wave preflights](preflight.md). Planning snapshots do not lock claims or establish runtime readiness.

## Products

| Aim | Release | Outcome / design case | Jobs | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P01 | V1 | Background reports and imports / C2/C8 | ELZ-010, RC-12, RC-13, RC-42 | Durable work/result primitives; consumer supplies report logic, endpoints and deployment. RC-42 (durable operation deduplication) is later. |
| P02 | V1 | Webhook and API-driven work / C1/C7 | ELZ-011, ELZ-023, RC-12, RC-42, RC-43 | Consumer supplies inbound HTTP/auth/signature verification; ready HTTP is an outbound recipe. RC-42 (durable operation deduplication) and RC-43 (outbox dispatch) are later. |
| P03 | V1 | Human review and approval queues / C5 | ELZ-024, ELZ-026, RC-10, RC-11, RC-12, RC-19 | Consumer supplies UI and business acceptance; approval persistence belongs to the owning Run. |
| P04 | V1 | Tenant invitation and feature access / C1 | RC-06, RC-07, RC-09, RC-19, RC-42 | Consumer supplies product roles/UI and identity deployment; paid subscription synchronization is V2. RC-06 (identity provider), RC-09 (external authorization provider) and RC-42 (durable operation deduplication) are later. |
| P05 | V1 | Quota-controlled asynchronous services / C2 | ELZ-022, RC-07, RC-08, RC-12, RC-13 | Allocation and execution budgets; no invoice or strict provider monetary cap without provider enforcement. |
| P06 | V1 | Scheduled research and notification / C7 | ELZ-027, ELZ-030, RC-02, RC-16, RC-18, RC-19, RC-12, ELZ-022, ELZ-101 | One selected provider, bounded retrieved context, explicit partial/unknown results; not research truth. |
| P07 | V1 | Bounded agent delegation with review / C6 | RC-11, RC-12, RC-15, RC-17, RC-18, ELZ-022, ELZ-024, ELZ-030 | Consumer owns acceptance and allowed tools; durable cost settlement and general resume are not inferred. RC-17 (external agent runner) is later. |
| P08 | V1 | Artifact upload and transformation / C8 | RC-12, RC-13, RC-14, RC-42 | Consumer supplies transformation and ingress; storage/stream bounds are tested at named boundaries. RC-42 (durable operation deduplication) is later. |
| P09 | V2 | Metered multi-provider AI services / C9 | RC-08, RC-16, RC-21, RC-22, RC-23, RC-25, ELZ-100 | Consumer owns commercial prices, tax/invoicing and settlement policy; more than one provider needs conformance evidence. |
| P10 | V2 | Internal transfers and reconciled refunds / C3/C4 | ELZ-031, RC-22, RC-24, RC-42, RC-43 | The first release has provider-neutral payment lifecycle and checkout, not a payment provider. RC-24 (payment provider), RC-42 (durable operation deduplication) and RC-43 (outbox dispatch) are later. Consumer supplies accounting policy; no bank/compliance certification or inventory system. |
| P11 | V2 | Event-driven and incremental build workflows / new named workload | RC-27, RC-28, RC-29, RC-30 | The first release supplies no part of P11. RC-27 (broker/pubsub), RC-28 (resequencing), RC-29 (incremental build graph) and RC-30 (foreign consumer protocol) are later; a product claim waits for a next release. For later P11 work: one broker/language; cache keys include actual build dependencies. Not a general build service. |
| P12 | V3 | Offline collaboration, devices and classified records / research-gated | RC-33, RC-34, RC-35, RC-36 | Separate admission decisions; these directions require additional domain contracts and are not a release promise yet. |
| P13 | V1B | Recipe-driven bot platform: run engine, agent pattern and Lua recipes (waves 1 to 3) / new named workload; extends C6/C7 | ELZ-114, RC-44, RC-45, RC-46, RC-47, RC-12, RC-48, RC-49, RC-50, RC-51, RC-52, RC-69, RC-53, RC-54, RC-55, RC-56, RC-57, RC-58, RC-70, RC-59, RC-60, RC-61, RC-62, RC-63, RC-64, RC-65, RC-66, RC-67 | Consumer owns the bot product: business records, public API, screens, channels, tenancy policy and deployment. Waves 4 and later are named in bot-platform.md and are not registered. |

## Catalogue accounting

Baseline status is immutable inventory history, not a current planning status. Ready summaries below are checked against their actual manifests. Primary owner is a planning responsibility, not exhaustive authorship.

| ID | Baseline | Release / stage | Primary owner / product aims | Disposition and scoped Contract / gap |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| assembly/run-host | not in baseline (new row, ELZ-421) | V2 / later | ELZ-421 / P09 | planned-contract: Planned Assembly that hosts assembly/run-engine Runs on one node: provisioning with one evidence namespace, serialized lease renewal (ELZ-422), text AI bindings with a trusted outcome route, predecessor fencing before recovery, deadline wakes from committed deadlines (ELZ-423), and reap after terminal reads. |
| [delivery/durable-mailbox](../../pieces/delivery/durable-mailbox/piece.json) | planned | V1B / pieces | ELZ-344 / P13 | ready-contract: Durable mail and room continuation chains over a consumer-owned Ecto Repo on PostgreSQL 19 in three Piece-owned tables (chains, links, messages) that Migration.install/1 creates and protects with the state/tenant-isolation floor. new/2 binds a declaration of 1..64 message names, each built with core/payload-schema, and its identity {id, version, SHA-256 of the canonical JSON}; verify/1 runs the floor check per table. open/3 creates a chain and its first link (equal bytes and declaration duplicate, else conflict; a held run_id or unit_identity is identity_in_use). put/3 refuses, in order and before any write, a bad shape, an undeclared name, another schema version, a payload that fails the schema, a caller transaction, another isolation level, an unknown chain, another declaration, a stored message_id with other bytes (equal bytes duplicate, wherever addressed) and a chain at max_pending open messages; mail to any link's run_id goes to the chain. Every write after open/3 takes the chain row FOR UPDATE first: claim/3 gives the delivering message again, else stopped, else the oldest pending message to the live tip; settle/3 records applied, not_applied (pending again) or one of four refusals (record_size also stops the link), repeats are duplicate or not_delivering; plan_rollover/3 records the successor's pin on the tip; rollover/3 closes the tip and inserts exactly one successor that keeps the pin unless an explicit change, refusing unknown_link, already_rolled_over (equal bytes repeat the answer), delivery_unsettled, plan_mismatch and identity_in_use. chain/3 pages links (1..256) with the tip and its carry; status/3 reads a message's state. Canonical JSON (compact Jason, keys sorted by bytes) decides every comparison and the 65,536-byte bounds; fixed error atoms, log: false, no telemetry, no Run start, engine call or timer. |
| [workflow/turn-policy](../../pieces/workflow/turn-policy/piece.json) | planned | V1B / pieces | ELZ-345 / P13 | ready-contract: Pure pattern step in the core/operator shape over PatternStep v1 neutral maps for the root callable of a room Run: the rules addressed (lead-bot and mention-only presets, host-vouched reply-to), round_robin, bounded everyone and decided (one decision action whose untrusted pick falls back to the default member), and a pure host-rule behaviour that fails closed, its context and rule state bounded in bytes and to 30 levels of nesting; every rule keeps one open turn, members only, the max_bot_turns bound (also for bot and peer messages), escalation, handoff, a bounded window, the rollover carry and unique action IDs; check_start/2 (which also refuses a carry whose queue or bot_turns passes the bound), restart_carry/1 and pick_schema/0. No clock, I/O, store, process or model call. |
| [workflow/delegation](../../pieces/workflow/delegation/piece.json) | planned | V1B / pieces | ELZ-346 / P13 | ready-contract: Pure child-Run grant over trusted parent facts: refuses the whole request in order malformed, revoked (root, parent, child or any lineage bot), stale (an agent parent below the root's auth_epoch), depth over max_depth 1..8, cycle (the root's ID or a lineage bot), escalation past the parent's scopes or spaces, budget (not 1..budget_remaining) and version_change (a change without a recorded-action ref); then delegates the child from the root with domain/principal (one level), passes the parent's validated effective authority as the domain/authorization parent bound, the inherited or explicitly changed pin, depth, the lineage with the child and a parent-close rule (abandon, request_cancel by default, cancel); on_parent_close/1 maps each open child of an ended parent to abandon, one elzoo.delegation.cancel_requested v1 request, or an Attempt cancel. Errors are the fixed atoms malformed, revoked, stale, depth, cycle, escalation, budget and version_change, or {:principal, atom} with domain/principal's reason unchanged; none echoes an ID, scope or space. No I/O, clock, process, store, Run start, cancel or message. |
| [delivery/room](../../pieces/delivery/room/piece.json) | planned | V1B / pieces | ELZ-348 / P13 | ready-contract: Durable Assembly in plain Elixir with no table, process, timer or state of its own that hosts a room as the continuation chain of delivery/durable-mailbox, one assembly/run-engine Run per link on a fresh Unit room/<sha256(tenant NUL chain)>/<seq> with the workflow/turn-policy root pattern and its version pinned in state/recipe-store. open/3 checks the start (check_start/2, link 1, no carry, the reserve) before it pins, opens the chain and starts the first Run, and after a conflicting open, also a repeated one, removes the request's pin for the first Run ID unless that chain's first link holds the same Run and pin. After failure, including a crash between attempts, the host repeats the same open/3 request, not resume/3, until it receives {:ok, _}, {:error, {:mailbox, :conflict}} after release, or a definite step 1 or step 3 refusal. deliver/3 gives one mail input only to an idle tip, refuses mail of the room's own schemas or of a turn-ID form, and settles each message by the engine reply and the classification read after it (record_size stops the link, definite engine refusals are refused, lost replies, exits and halts stay delivering); it gives the rollover message instead when the slot count, request count or clock leaves less than the per-message reserve (B turns of the declared bot worst case with their posts, one notice, and the decision worst case under decided or a host rule) plus the carry and finish. wake/3 routes the host timer and gives a delivering message again before the wake. rollover/3 of the link link_seq returns {:not_tip, seq} with nothing written when the tip is another link, then reads result/3 (a valid finished carry, else restart_carry/1 with the same checks), settles a claimed message from transcript/3, unpins an earlier plan of another version, plans and pins, takes the mailbox's atomic step (a plan_mismatch unpins its own pin), starts the successor and unpins the old Run; resume/3 finishes a stopped sequence. Every call can be repeated with the same request; fixed tagged errors, no retry, log or telemetry. |

| [assembly/ledger-checkout](../../pieces/assembly/ledger-checkout/piece.json) | deferred | V2 / pieces | ELZ-378 / P10 | ready-contract: Durable checkout saga over a consumer-owned Ecto Repo on PostgreSQL 19 under the state/tenant-isolation floor (checkouts and steps tables, no DELETE), one Assembly with its own log (Q10 A) and no provider named (Q9 D): pay an invoice (charge through domain/payment-lifecycle create/get/charge with payment_id checkout.<id>, create/3 with the approved terms on every pass and an existing payment of another amount or recipient approval_mismatch, never adopted, domain/invoice apply_payment with payment_ref checkout/<id>, domain/journal post checkout/<id>/settle) or top up a wallet (charge, journal post checkout/<id>/wallet, optional host allowance grant Need, optionally one declared parallel group of wallet and allowance); open/3 checks every field against the bound of the Piece that takes it before storing, idempotent by checkout_id; each step written started before its first call, its result and compensation in one checkout transaction, every Piece or Need call outside it; a :pending or :unknown charge waits :charge_intent with the admission and nothing registered, charge/3's {:state, _} re-reads get/3; a definite failure compensates the succeeded steps in reverse position order (comp/charge refund of :remaining waiting :refund_intent, reverse_payment, journal reverse with the fixed reason, revoke Need), each idempotent by its key; a failed compensation is compensation_failed and stops needs_operator; a Piece's :unavailable or a Need's other answer waits :retry with nothing recorded, :in_transaction and :isolation are resume/3's own error; resume/3 holds a session advisory lock per checkout on one connection checked out with timeout :infinity for the whole call and a caller without it calls nothing and waits :busy; a parallel group starts both members and is decided when both are definite; list_running/3 bounded, for open or needs_operator checkouts. Fixed error atoms that echo no ID or amount. |

| [delivery/aggregation](../../pieces/delivery/aggregation/piece.json) | planned | V1 / pieces | ELZ-098 / P07 | ready-contract on task branch; task acceptance separate: Bounded correlated join over a declared set of branch identities. accept/2 validates each core/envelope DeliveryResult, attributes it through a configured {:field, path} branch_key (correlation_id, attempt_id or a declared value field — never arrival position), and records first-wins branch outcomes: completed→fulfilled, rejected/failed→failed, timed_out→timed_out, unknown→unavailable. mark_lost/3 records first-class :timed_out/:killed/:unavailable losses, not synthetic results. decision/2 is a pure {join, outcome} transition over pending/all/partial/failed/expired; expiry converts pending branches to :missing and late branches to :timed_out and closes. Post-deadline and post-close input is recorded late, never merged; on-time duplicates are counted and differing outcome/value digests mark the branch conflicted. State is a plain map bounded by declared count and per-branch encoded identity/result size, retained redacted (SHA-256 value_digest, never payloads) for caller persistence. Elzoo.Aggregation.Runner fans out declared {branch_id, fun} pairs at most once per runner call under an injected Task.Supervisor, after validating capacity for every synthetic failure shape: exceptions and invalid returns become sanitized synthetic failed results, mismatched result identities fail only the originating branch, undispatchable starts become rejected/not_dispatched, join-refused results become :unavailable losses, and the rearmed deadline marks survivors :timed_out; normal stop/supervised shutdown clean tracked tasks without reversing Effects. Durable fan-out, sagas, scheduling, retries and Reactor are out of scope. |

| [state/artifact](../../pieces/state/artifact/piece.json) | deferred | V1 / pieces | ELZ-096 / P01, P05, P06, P07, P08, P11, P12 | ready-contract on task branch; task acceptance separate: Tenant-owned, versioned artifact references in the ready Store whose bytes live in the ready integration/object-store Piece: stage, commit, abort, put, new_version, head, get, list, versions, delete, reap and status. One Store stream artifact/<tenant>/<artifact_id> of self-contained non_state_bearing records — artifact_staged, artifact_committed and artifact_deleted each carry the full version record plus resulting state/state_at, with no inference of complete history from surviving rows — under head-revision CAS, plus a best-effort tenant index stream artifact-index/<tenant>. Content hash is SHA-256 over <<hash_format::8, bytes>>, the declared positive format stamped per record and used for verification on every get; a format change is a declared migration. Object keys are artifacts/<tenant>/<artifact_id>/v<version>/<sha256>; tenant and artifact_id are validated to a safe segment before key construction. Every operation presents caller %{tenant, principal, scopes} as consumer-vouched attribution: read paths enforce the recorded access terms and access_expires_at_unix_ms (a hash never grants access), versions filters each returned record by its own terms after the head gate, mutating operations require caller.tenant == record.tenant which the tenant-scoped stream makes structural (plus the recorded terms for delete), and cross-tenant lookup returns :not_found. Appending to an artifact with a committed head enforces that head's recorded terms and access_expires_at_unix_ms: new_version checks before staging and commit re-checks before every staging-to-committed transition, so a denied caller cannot append a head through put, new_version or manual stage+commit; with no committed head appends follow the tenant rule and the first commit establishes the face, and a caller who passes the head's terms may declare the new version's access (narrowing or widening is their declaration). Artifact and index streams require low_water == 0 and validated sequence coverage through the sampled committed Store revision; any retained prefix, missing or malformed row, inconsistent version or illegal transition fails closed as {:store_damaged, info} before object reads, uploads or Store writes. Self-contained rows and contiguous version numbers do not establish complete history after retention. stage reserves the largest encoded lifecycle payload, including the maximum valid state_at timestamp width, before upload. commit verifies object presence, stored transport checksum and size through reconcile_put before the Store write; an unknown put persists an upload: :uncertain staging record resolved by verification, never blind re-put; a Store {:conflict, _} is {:commit_failed, %{expected, observed}} and an indeterminate write is {:commit_failed, :indeterminate}. Object deletes on abort/delete/reap are best-effort with reported outcomes; every already-deleted version's bytes are deletion-attempted again under reap's cleanup result, so a refused/unknown delete is retried by the next reap; list propagates storage and damage failures as errors, never a partial page; reap is caller-driven. Creation, access-expiry and retention fields use the published created_at_unix_ms, access_expires_at_unix_ms and retain_until_unix_ms names in input, public records and persisted JSON. |

A revision chain fences settlement, delete and redefine chunks from concurrent decisions; a changed head stops the operation as :contended. Dispatch rechecks the current generation and occurrence before and after enqueue, suppresses terminal work, and expires overdue dispatch-pending occurrences before enqueue. Each admitted occurrence dispatches as one Oban job through integration/jobs whose job_key is the encoded occurrence id (at-least-once, advisory uniqueness); the dispatch outcome is persisted through a fenced append after the slot batch commits. reap/2 runs the same settlement reconcile for every unsettled occurrence before the TTL split — a terminal Oban state wins over expiry — then releases occurrences older than occurrence_ttl_ms, records them expired and cancels their jobs. delete/2 releases unsettled occurrences through chunked fenced appends, then commits the deletion and retires the index. Optional messages: :while_active (the only value; absent = the engine without it, byte for byte; recorded in the configure control and in the fingerprint, wrapped after expiry_reports, so adding or removing it on restart is incompatible_recovery) gives a message input to the root pattern only, never a nested callable, when the lifecycle decides PermitWork or RemainWaiting, also while slots are active, as one ordinary pattern call that settles no slot, in the fold step of its own record after its own expiry pass, with every other condition of an input that settles no slot (generation open, no stop pending, not paused, root open, room for its invocation entry) unchanged; wake, start and resource_event keep their rule. Read-only transcript/3 returns one Run's committed history in commit order (inputs, gate and authorization verdicts, admitted actions with their callable path, nested starts and kept finishes, settlements with their record kind, reason and actual, later actuals and the terminal outcome), each entry derived from the folded state change of one committed record with its own recorded time, with the call path, scope, limits, reader configuration, reading, budgets, rechecks and errors of event_waits; it calls no callback, mutates nothing and emits no telemetry. The owner recheck of result/3, event_waits/3 and transcript/3 compares the Run's own facts (id, outcome, operation_id, current_attempt, completion_revision and evidence_ref), not the revision of the owner's whole stream, so an owner command that leaves them equal, of another Run of a shared owner or of this Run, does not fail the read; one that changes one of them during an event_waits/3 or transcript/3 read gives unavailable. |

change/3 takes effect at the next renewal without proration; cancel/3 at :period_end fixes cancel_ms at the end of the period running at now_ms (the start of a period renewed ahead that has not started, revoking its grant there and withdrawing its fee line) and at :now cancels with one revocation at now_ms for each grant not yet expired (after a period-end cancel inside the ahead window keeping its earlier revocation and withdrawn fee line), storing the result as last_cancel for get/3; mark_past_due/3 and mark_paid/3 take the host's invoice reading; every transition names the expected revision (stale_revision, then {:state, state}); fixed error atoms that echo no ID; a database failure is unavailable and safe to retry. | [domain/invoice](../../pieces/domain/invoice/piece.json) | planned | V2 / pieces | ELZ-367 / P09, P10 | ready-contract: Numbered invoices of one currency in plain Ecto/Postgrex tables on PostgreSQL 19 under the state/tenant-isolation floor (five tables: invoices UNIQUE (tenant, series, number), lines, applications, reversals keyed by reversal_id, series with the last number). An invoice is drafted for one billing account, currency and series (create/3 idempotent by invoice_id), takes up to 256 lines idempotent by line_id: a :usage line of the total of a complete domain/pricing charge (incomplete_charge otherwise; source_ref card_id/version/period), a :fee line of a domain/plan fee line taken as data (source_ref subscription_id/index) and :adjustment lines, all in the invoice's currency (currency otherwise), never negative on an invoice. finalize/3 locks the invoice row (for a credit note first the corrected invoice's), then the series row (created on first use with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING), takes the series' next number from 1 and freezes the lines in the same transaction, so committed finalizations of one series have consecutive numbers, a rolled-back one uses none and a void keeps its number; an invoice becomes :open, or :paid when its payable is zero; a non-zero total names the domain/journal operation invoice/<invoice_id>/issue as a pending posting, a zero total none; a repeat returns the same number and operation ID with duplicate true. mark_posted/3 records the host's journal result. apply_payment/3 applies a positive amount to an :open invoice up to its payable (total minus issued credit notes), idempotent by payment_ref, and makes it :paid at the payable. reverse_payment/3 takes back an amount, or :remaining fixed in its row, of one application up to what of it is still applied (over_reversal, nothing_applied, unknown_payment), idempotent by its own reversal_id, and reopens a :paid invoice whose paid drops below the payable. void/3 voids a :draft or :open document with nothing paid and nothing credited at the expected revision. credit_note/3 drafts a credit note of 1..256 negative adjustment lines of an :open or :paid invoice within its payable, checked again at its finalize/3 under the corrected invoice's row lock; issued, it grows the corrected invoice's credited and may make it :paid; get/3 shows an excess of paid over the payable as credit_balance. Every write checks shape (invalid), then the caller's transaction (in_transaction) before any query, runs in its own transaction at a checked READ COMMITTED Need (isolation) and locks the invoice row first; amounts are stored as Elzoo.Money.Codec bytes and summed with domain/money only. Fixed error atoms that echo no ID or amount; a database failure is unavailable. No journal post, tax, discount, rendering or multi-currency invoice. |
| [integration/ai-text-google](../../pieces/integration/ai-text-google/piece.json) | planned | V1B / pieces | ELZ-373 / P13 | ready-contract: Gemini API generateContent provider of integration/ai-text with an API key, through req_llm 1.31's documented google provider-callback API, for the evidenced model gemini-3.8-flash only (other models refused invalid_request): max_retries 0, one HTTP exchange with the body bounded to 4 MiB of wire bytes while received and every response halted by an Elzoo step (no redirect, no retry, no req_llm error value); key only from the injected Need, sent only in x-goog-api-key; features tools, structured output and image input (inline_data, png, jpeg, webp, gif); sampling, stop sequences, reasoning and cache hints not declared; system messages only before the others, one systemInstruction part each; function calls and responses carry the neutral call id, a failed tool result as the response's error key; each tool's parameter schema unchanged in parametersJsonSchema, a root other than type object refused unsupported_feature; structured output as responseMimeType application/json with responseJsonSchema unchanged, a schema with a keyword outside the reference's supported list (oneOf excluded, as the reference reads it as anyOf), a $ref other than #/$defs/<name>, $id or $defs below the root, or a cyclic $ref refused unsupported_feature; structured output with tools, a final model turn and a final Gemini turn (the trailing user and tool-result messages) without non-empty text, unless it is tool results only, refused; finish STOP stop or tool_calls, MAX_TOKENS length, SAFETY, PROHIBITED_CONTENT, BLOCKLIST, SPII and IMAGE_SAFETY refusal (also as a prompt blockReason), other reasons {:other, reason}; usage from usageMetadata with output candidates plus thoughts and absent counters :unknown; status table 400 provider_invalid_request (400 with ErrorInfo reason API_KEY_INVALID authentication_failed), 401, 403, 404, 413, 429 throttled, 503 overloaded, 504 provider_timeout, other provider_error; retry_after for 429 and 503 from Retry-After, else RetryInfo retryDelay rounded up, nil over 86,400 s or malformed. |
| [integration/ai-text-compatible](../../pieces/integration/ai-text-compatible/piece.json) | planned | V1B / pieces | ELZ-374 / P13 | ready-contract: OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions provider of integration/ai-text at one HTTPS base URL per endpoint: endpoint/1 takes exactly base_url (https, 1..2048 bytes, no userinfo, query or fragment), an egress policy, its resolver Need, a transport Need given %{host, port, addresses} returning {:ok, req_options} and a model table of 1..64 unique ids of 1..256 bytes of UTF-8 without a control character (C0, DEL or C1) with features from tools, structured_output, stop_sequences, sampling and image_input; shape refusals are :invalid with no resolver call, then one Elzoo.EgressPolicy.resolve/3 gives {:egress, reason} or an endpoint holding the checked host, port and addresses (fixed for its lifetime; inspect shows no Need). translate/2 is pure: a non-endpoint term or a model outside the table invalid_request, a feature the table does not declare for the model unsupported_feature (replayed tool calls or tool results count as tools), a tool result with is_error true unsupported_feature, more than 4 stop sequences invalid_request, else a Chat Completions body through req_llm 1.31's LM Studio provider with each image as a data: URL. call/2 makes exactly one exchange: the transport Need's options (a failing, raising or non-keyword answer is {:transport, :unavailable} before any byte, classified transport by ai-text) replace req_llm's dropped finch option (a host finch option keeps its pool but not its own receive_timeout), then retry false, redirect false and the receive timeout, max_retries 0, the key only in the authorization header, every response halted by an Elzoo step with the body bounded to 4 MiB of wire bytes while received. decode/2 reads the documented chat.completion object (one choice whose message has role assistant and a content key; refusal, stop, length, tool_calls, content_filter, other finishes), usage from the documented usage object with absent fields :unknown, and the status table of integration/ai-text-openai with Retry-After seconds for 429 and 503. |

### RC-01

Versioned stored-state compatibility

**Product aims:** P01, P02, P04, P08, P10.

**Contract:** Versioned stored record -> current record or explicit incompatible/unwritable result. The owning consumer chooses supported versions; older writers cannot silently discard fields. Do not retrofit the active durable-job contract.

**Acceptance:** Frozen writer corpora, frozen older-reader fixture, unknown version, lossless migration and unwritable older-writer checks. Missing/changed golden bytes fail checks.

**Reuse:** Existing encoding and provider migration facilities; pure upcast policy only where missing.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Accepted persisted formats, supported readers/writers and first real evolution need; no placeholder v2 or widened ready ID.

### RC-02

Persistent schedule recipe

**Product aims:** P06.

**Contract:** Schedule identity, generation and instant -> durable occurrence admission. Time zone/DST/misfire rules explicit; occurrence identity survives restart. Start with one provider and bounded recurring policy.

**Acceptance:** Restart, duplicate occurrence, missed interval, clock boundary and declared skip/queue/replace behavior. Timer recipe alone cannot satisfy this job.

**Reuse:** Oban/AshOban candidate; exercise provider persistence and scheduling boundary.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- One persistent schedule API with timezone, DST, misfire and occurrence transaction choice; timer availability is insufficient.

### RC-03

Redacted telemetry and one exporter

**Product aims:** P01, P05, P07. Supporting policy, evidence, operations, discoverability or later example; not an additional product prerequisite or a delivered end-to-end product.

**Contract:** Versioned event schema, allowed fields and cardinality -> observations through one named exporter. Observations are not durable audit or accounting records.

**Acceptance:** Secret-bearing payload redaction, bounded labels, exporter failure/drop behavior and attach/detach lifecycle. No payloads or credentials in emitted records.

**Reuse:** :telemetry and OpenTelemetry; direct recipes wherever sufficient.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Named exporter API and bounded/drop/redaction contract; no exporter adopted.

### RC-04

Operational interfaces for new stateful Pieces

**Product aims:** P01, P05, P07. Supporting policy, evidence, operations, discoverability or later example; not an additional product prerequisite or a delivered end-to-end product.

**Contract:** Typed config/control/readiness responses with declared authorization and configuration revision. Applicability is per Piece. Stop, pause and business drain remain distinct; ready runtimes are not silently extended.

**Acceptance:** Unauthorized command, stale config revision and unsupported command rejection. A drain promise requires a named work-completion check; otherwise report unsupported.

**Reuse:** OTP lifecycle and existing operational-contract schemas; no universal management service.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-05

Scoped credential references

**Product aims:** P01, P03, P04, P05, P06, P07, P08, P09, P10, P11, P12.

**Contract:** Opaque secret reference plus caller scope -> ephemeral credential or explicit denial/unavailable. Select injected credentials as initial backend; document refresh and rotation semantics without promising a vault.

**Acceptance:** Wrong scope, missing secret, rotation boundary and error/log redaction; secret material never enters receipts or model context.

**Reuse:** Consumer-owned environment/mounted-secret backend first; vault plugins later, separately tested.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Consumer credential injection API, scope and rotation semantics must be admitted.

### RC-06

Authenticated identity and principal mapping

**Product aims:** P04, P05, P09, P12.

**Contract:** Verified provider identity (issuer, subject, audience) -> stable scoped principal. Authentication and account linking remain provider contracts; no custom password or token protocol.

**Acceptance:** Wrong issuer/audience, expired/revoked session, ambiguous linking and tenant crossover; supplied principal strings are not authentication evidence.

**Reuse:** Evaluate AshAuthentication/OIDC ecosystem at one selected boundary; pin and test exact adoption before ready.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Select authentication provider/API and issuer/audience/revocation/linking rules; trusted actor maps are not authenticated identities.

### RC-07

Membership, invitations and feature entitlement

**Product aims:** P04, P05, P09.

**Contract:** Two distinct Pieces: single-use tenant invitation/membership transition; versioned feature entitlement with explicit revocation/grace. Atomic storage owns membership changes; billing provider synchronization is outside V1.

**Acceptance:** Concurrent invitation acceptance, cross-tenant token replay, revoked inviter, changed role and entitlement revoked while work is queued. No partial membership activation.

**Reuse:** Ash resource/policy plus selected transactional storage; no new identity framework.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Choose invitation and entitlement storage transaction with concurrency/revocation evidence.

### RC-08

Durable tenant allocation

**Product aims:** P05, P09.

**Contract:** Tenant, unit, period and operation identity -> atomic reservation/settlement/reset. Reuse arithmetic but keep quota state and reset authority separate from a Run budget.

**Acceptance:** Concurrent last-unit admission, duplicate settle, reset race, restart with open reservation and unknown usage; missing usage cannot become zero.

**Reuse:** Evaluate provider transactional counters; compose budget arithmetic, never copy a second reservation engine.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Choose atomic reservation/settlement/reset transaction; budget owner alone is volatile.

### RC-09

One authorization-provider translation

**Product aims:** P04.

**Contract:** Translate one provider policy decision into the existing authorization contract; policy version and unavailable/denied distinction retained. Provider output never bypasses exact-action approval.

**Acceptance:** Unavailable provider fails closed, unknown decision, changed policy and child authority restriction; direct versus translated policy scenarios agree within declared scope.

**Reuse:** Ash policy boundary first; Bodyguard/Cedar only when the selected contract needs them.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Select one policy provider and total unavailable/unknown mapping.

### RC-10

Transactional retained decision records

**Product aims:** P01, P02, P03, P04, P05, P06, P07, P08, P09, P10, P11, P12.

**Contract:** Decision and actor/resource/policy attribution commit atomically under one operation identity. Retention and access declared; telemetry is not the backing store. Select storage supporting the actual joint transaction.

**Acceptance:** Crash between decision and audit, duplicate write, redaction and unauthorized read; if the existing Store cannot provide joint atomicity, create a reviewed adapter/contract prerequisite instead of weakening the invariant.

**Reuse:** Existing event records or transactional Ecto/Ash storage after exact-boundary evaluation; no tamper-proof claim.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Joint decision/audit atomicity is unresolved. ELZ-009 Store is not a general multi-record business transaction; caller transactions are refused (ELZ-032).

### RC-11

Durable intent and acceptance

**Product aims:** P03, P07.

**Contract:** Work Item has stable intent, acceptance revision, assignee/authority and explicit lifecycle. A Run result is evidence offered for acceptance, never automatic business completion.

**Acceptance:** Stale holder cannot accept, duplicate completion is idempotent, no-op writes nothing, dependency failure policy explicit, cancellation of an Attempt does not erase intent.

**Reuse:** Ash resource/action/state facilities; reuse provider ownership when ELZ-028 determines it suffices.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Durable Work Item lifecycle and atomic acceptance/ownership transaction not admitted.

### RC-12

Run and Attempt lifecycle with durable approvals

**Product aims:** P01, P02, P03, P05, P06, P07, P08, P11, P13.

**Contract:** Run/Attempt identity, current owner, reciprocal parent-child links, cancellation and effect settlement remain distinct. Persist approval inputs and re-evaluate current authority before dispatch; provider sessions are external references. Boundary with the bot platform track (P13): this job owns Run and Attempt identity, the parent-child links, the Run outcome set, the rule of which Attempt is current and what a cancel names, and approval persistence. At dispatch it calls ready workflow/approval, which already runs the decision again against current context; this job does not write that check a second time. The admitted design keeps these rules. (a) The release event is durable before a dispatch is started. (b) A key is released one time: with a release record the only check is Elzoo.Approval.revalidate/3, and :already_released from release/4 is never a permission. (c) With no release record the Run restores what it has: an approval that has no evidence yet stays an open wait and is not refused; with stored evidence it calls Elzoo.Approval.release/4 with that fresh evidence, for each scope; it passes :retained only after select_retained chose an existing retained consent. (d) revalidate/3 knows nothing about dispatch: :ok is never proof that nothing was sent and never permits a send by itself. (e) When delivery/effect-intent holds a reservation for the action, the action counts as possibly sent: this job starts no new dispatch for it, its outcome comes from the reconciliation of delivery/effect-intent, and this job never makes a replacement action for it. (f) After a refusal or an error of release/4 or revalidate/3 this job starts no new dispatch; that is not a claim that no send occurs, because a task that was already scheduled can still send, and that fence is a Need of the host. (g) Each restart window ends in a defined outcome, the window with an admitted intent and no attempt included. RC-48 owns only the phase with open waits and pause and applies the Attempt rule of this job without a second definition, and RC-50 calls this job and does not repeat it.

**Acceptance:** Restart, late child result, duplicate completion, unknown spawn/cancel outcome, parent wakeup loss, stale Attempt and restored approval bypass. Child death observed; completion requires durable evidence. Approval restart windows, as composition tests with a recording Store, a valid current context, host activation and a fake Adapter that counts sends: an approval that has no evidence yet is an open wait after a restart; a crash before the release event is durable runs release/4 again with the stored fresh evidence, for scope :once, :run and :root each, and evidence that is expired or too old by then gives a refusal; a crash after the release event is durable and before the intent is admitted runs revalidate/3 and starts a dispatch only on :ok, with one intent and one send at the end; a crash after the intent is admitted and before a dispatch ends in one dispatch or in a defined not-sent outcome, never in a stuck Run; a crash after the dispatch and before its outcome is recorded gives no second intent and no second send, also when revalidate/3 answers :ok, and the outcome is reconciled or stays unknown; in each run the Store holds the release event before the first record of the intent. With a recorded release, each of these gives a refusal and no new dispatch: the policy identity changed (:policy_changed); the current decision, action or escrow changed (:requirement_changed, :payload_changed, deny_at_release); the authority of the approver no longer covers the escrowed writes (:approver_not_authorized); the evidence is expired (:expired), too old (:evidence_too_old) or revoked (:revoked). These named reasons are the cases; 'each change of authority' is not a case. :already_released from release/4 starts no dispatch. This job makes no replacement action for an action whose outcome is unknown.

**Reuse:** Evaluate Oban/Jido/Reactor boundaries before implementing orchestration remainder; no mandatory global runtime. workflow/approval (ELZ-024) already has both restart calls: release/4 refuses a key that has a release record (:already_released, gate g_unreleased in approval.cure), and revalidate/3 checks a recorded release again against current context, writes nothing, answers :ok whether or not the writes were sent, and gives :not_released when no record exists (approval.ex, README section Restore). Fresh evidence of scope :run or :root makes the retained consent inside the successful release event, and :retained only selects an existing consent and gives :no_retained_consent when none exists (approval.cure new_consent; README, Retained consent and scope). workflow/approval does not claim exactly-once dispatch of the released writes (README, Not claimed). delivery/effect-intent (ELZ-023): the intent key is the SHA-256 of all five identity fields (model.ex), and the same identity with different arguments or binding refuses startup; a timeout, a task death and a recovery are unknown outcomes and never permission to send again; a recovered dispatched reservation never schedules a local send; a paused old task can still perform its reserved send; only a verified no-effect resolution permits a separately authorized new logical request, and a new key otherwise risks a duplicate (README). This job calls them and writes no check of its own.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Durable Run/Attempt and approval persistence/current-authority interface not admitted; ELZ-010 job Run is a narrower Contract.
- RC-10 (decision and attribution committed atomically) has no output left, and ELZ-055 gives the audit log only. If this job needs that atomic commit, the admission names its owner.
- The restart protocol between workflow/approval and delivery/effect-intent is not designed in this text. The admission fixes it together with RC-50, under rules (a) to (g) of the contract: the call order, the persisted fields, the outcome of each restart window, how an unknown outcome is shown to the pattern and to an approver, and the check against delivery/effect-intent that an intent with no attempt started no send.

### RC-13

Object storage and owned artifacts

**Product aims:** P01, P05, P06, P07, P08, P11, P12.

**Contract:** Separate provider byte-storage translation from tenant-owned versioned artifact references. Versioned canonical content hash, size, access, retention and expiry are explicit; a hash never grants access.

**Acceptance:** Partial upload cleanup, wrong hash, stale access URL, retention expiry, cross-tenant lookup and failed write/reference commit. Verify integrity on read under declared format.

**Reuse:** S3-compatible client and local compatible service candidate; consumer bucket and credentials. AWS-specific guarantees require separate evidence.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Select byte store API and byte/reference commit, integrity and access boundary.

### RC-14

Bounded incremental transfer

**Product aims:** P06, P07, P08, P09, P11.

**Contract:** Stream chunks carry explicit completion, cancellation and partial-result status. Bound buffering and declare who closes/drains on consumer failure.

**Acceptance:** Slow consumer, disconnect, cancellation, incomplete final chunk and end-of-stream flush; memory bound enforced in named scenarios.

**Reuse:** Elixir/OTP and maintained HTTP streaming APIs; ordinary Enum/Stream remains unchanged.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Choose streaming API, buffer ownership and failure/close semantics.

### RC-15

Correlated result joins

**Product aims:** P07.

**Contract:** Declared branch identities plus correlated results -> bounded join state and explicit all/partial/failed/expired decision. Arrival order is not identity; retention and duplicate policy explicit.

**Acceptance:** Duplicate result, absent/killed branch, late result, timeout and unreadable denial record. Each expected branch contributes at most once.

**Reuse:** Use ordinary collection operations; author only missing join and timeout policy.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-16

One model-provider boundary

**Product aims:** P06, P07, P09, P11.

**Contract:** Declared model capabilities and validated request -> result/stream/tool proposals plus source-tagged usage. Tool proposals remain inert data. Unsupported features fail explicitly; no universal provider equivalence.

**Acceptance:** Malformed tool arguments, interrupted stream, unknown usage, rate limit, timeout and unsupported structured output. Pin one provider/SDK and exercise real boundary before claiming adoption.

**Reuse:** req_llm and maintained SDK candidates from existing study; no new HTTP client or tokenizer.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Select one model SDK/provider capability, usage provenance and stream API; credentials and exact-boundary tests required.

### RC-17

One external agent-runner boundary

**Product aims:** P07, P11.

**Contract:** Capability-declared start/status/cancel and optional resume; stable host tool registry id/version, current authority check and exact-action consent before execution. Provider session != Work Item or Run.

**Acceptance:** Unsupported resume, unknown tool, model self-approval, late output after cancellation, process death, unknown spawn outcome and credential redaction. Do not treat OS-process cleanup as a security sandbox.

**Reuse:** Choose one maintained agent/protocol SDK after fit check; ELZ-029 for actual CLI processes only.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Select runner API/SDK and actual resume/cancel/tool capabilities; unknown spawn remains unknown.

### RC-44

Typed payload check at a Port

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** One schema value per Port direction, keyed by the (schema_id, schema_version) that core/port already carries. check(payload) gives ok or a bounded list of violations with paths. The same schema value serves a module function, a recipe node, a tool argument and a model output. An unknown schema version is refused, not guessed. check never changes the payload: an unknown field is accepted or refused as the schema declares.

**Acceptance:** Valid and invalid payloads per declared type; an unknown field refused or accepted as declared, never dropped; oversized payload and deep nesting refused within a declared bound; violation list bounded; version mismatch refused; no payload content in errors or telemetry; a recorded list of the schemas on which jsv and ex_json_schema give different results.

**Reuse:** jsv, ex_json_schema and zoi are pinned in registry/providers.json. The two ready callers already differ: integration/ai-text validates with jsv, draft 2020-12, without casting (pieces/integration/ai-text/lib/elzoo/ai_text/schema.ex); integration/mcp-client validates with ex_json_schema (pieces/integration/mcp-client/lib/elzoo/mcp_client/snapshot.ex). Each returns one opaque refusal atom and no paths. Pick one validator here (jsv, unless the fit record gives a reason against it) and record the divergence. The ready Pieces keep their IDs, validators and results. Author only the Port binding and the bounded violation result. A thin wrapper alone earns no row: show the shared Port Contract that the four callers need.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Callers of this track get one validation result per value: RC-52 and RC-55 say which check decides.

### RC-45

Durable mode of a Unit

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A Unit that restores its state after a restart. Inputs arrive by a step call and by one declared info-message form. Each input is appended to state/event-log first, as its own record, and the pure core/operator transition reads an input only after that append. The result (state change and pending outbox items) is appended next under the CAS of the log Writer. On start the Unit folds the log; an input record with no result record goes to the transition again. state/checkpoint only shortens replay. Pending state/outbox items are offered again to a dispatcher that the caller declares; the CAS ack is the only acknowledgement point. Input identity is kept in the folded model within a declared retention bound, so a duplicate input inside the bound is not applied twice. Past the bound the Unit cannot tell a duplicate from a new input; the Contract states that limit, and the caller keeps its own guard (the engine does: an outcome for an action that is not open is late, RC-68). An append that the Writer answers with conflict means that another Unit holds the identity: the Unit stops with a named error that this Piece owns, and applies nothing. This Piece owns the one info-message form; a sender wraps its message in that form.

**Acceptance:** Crash before the input append (the sender gets no acknowledgement); after the input append and before the result append (the transition runs again and gives the same result); after the result append and before the outbox offer; during a checkpoint write. Replay gives the same state as the uninterrupted run for the listed scenarios. A duplicate input inside the retention bound is not applied twice; a test shows the stated limit past the bound. A Writer conflict stops the Unit with the named error and applies nothing. A checkpoint with another fold_version is not used and recovery replays the log. Recovery fails closed when the needed log prefix was pruned. A record with an unknown schema_version is refused. A transition that fails on a recorded input gives a declared stop, not a restart loop. Ready runtime/unit keeps its Contract unchanged.

**Reuse:** state/event-log, state/checkpoint and state/outbox are ready (ELZ-009). Of the three state Pieces, assembly/durable-job (ELZ-010) composes state/event-log and state/outbox, not state/checkpoint: mirror its Writer use and its key-in-fold dedup (submitter.ex), and do not edit it. durable-job keeps every key (memory grows with distinct keys, its stated limit), so the retention bound is new here. On a conflict the Writer reloads and answers {:conflict, observed}; it stops only if that reload fails (pieces/state/event-log/lib/elzoo/durable_log/writer.ex). core/operator (ELZ-002) is the transition shape. The version fields exist already: the record schema_version (pieces/state/event-log/lib/elzoo/durable_log.ex) and the checkpoint fold_version (pieces/state/checkpoint/lib/elzoo/checkpoint.ex); use them and refuse other versions, as durable-job does. Checkpoint.recover skips a checkpoint of another fold_version and falls back to replay. state/outbox supplies no dispatcher, scheduler or retry loop. delivery/idempotency is volatile and is not the dedup store here. Ready runtime/unit handles only synchronous step and snapshot calls and keeps its ID: this is a new Piece ID under the ready-Contract rule. No new storage engine.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Upcasting of stored state across format versions is RC-01 and is not a prerequisite: this Piece refuses an unknown version.

### RC-46

One supervised Unit per identity, with sleep and wake

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Start, find, stop and put to sleep one Unit for each stable identity (a Run, a room). At most one live Unit per identity on a node. A message for an identity goes to the info-message intake of its durable Unit (RC-45); a message to a sleeping identity wakes it through the durable mode. An idle Unit stops after a declared time. A declared maximum of live Units gives an explicit refusal. This Piece does not prevent a Unit for the same identity on a second node: the Store CAS of the log Writer arbitrates. On a conflict the Writer reloads and answers conflict; it stops only if that reload fails. So the durable Unit of RC-45 stops on that answer with the named error of RC-45. Shared runtime boundary: RC-45 owns exactly one business info-message form: `{:durable_unit_input, input_id, input}`. `input_id` is a caller-stable UTF-8 binary of 1..160 bytes without NUL, scoped to one durable stream; `input` is caller-owned data. A direct sender constructs that tuple; no second intake wrapper or Envelope is introduced. RC-45 applies the same intake semantics to `step(server, input_id, input)` and this tuple. Sending the tuple is not an acknowledgement or evidence of commit; mailbox delivery is volatile. Only the synchronous step's applied/duplicate reply establishes that the input's result is committed. The caller owns resubmission with the same identity after an uncertain outcome, and identity collisions are errors inside the retained window. RC-46 routes that tuple unchanged to the current local Unit for the identity. It must not report a successful send as durable acceptance, and must explicitly refuse a stop/wake race it cannot resolve; no durable mailbox or exactly-once delivery is promised. RC-47 owns no durable-unit tuple: it calls the supplied `wrap.(work_ref, outcome)` and sends that returned term to the owner's registered name, resolved when sending rather than retaining an owner PID. RC-47 tests use a receiver process, not RC-45. RC-50 supplies the wrap that builds the RC-45 tuple with an RC-68 input inside; it supplies stable input identity and engine-read time. A result for an action no longer open is late and is never applied, including after durable-unit input deduplication has expired. On engine recovery RC-50 records a failed outcome with a loss class for an open volatile local-work action before admitting subsequent results; a later result is late. Neither RC-45 nor RC-47 implements this engine policy. An append returning `{:conflict, observed}` causes RC-45 to stop with `{:shutdown, :durable_unit_conflict}`. The ready Writer normally reloads and returns conflict; RC-45, not the Writer or RC-46, owns the stop policy. No losing candidate state or outbox is installed or offered. A pure transition may already have been evaluated before a result-append conflict; “applies nothing” means no committed losing result, not that no CPU work occurred. RC-46 uses this exact stop reason for the losing Unit on another node. Local Registry uniqueness is not cluster ownership.

**Acceptance:** Two concurrent starts for one identity give one Unit; a message during stop wakes a new Unit or is refused explicitly, never lost silently; the live-Unit bound refuses with a named error; a crashed Unit restarts from its durable state; sleep and wake keep identity and state; a second Unit for one identity that loses the log CAS stops with the named error of RC-45 and applies nothing; no unbounded atom or process growth for the listed scenarios.

**Reuse:** OTP DynamicSupervisor and Registry, which docs/architecture.md and docs/dependencies.md already plan for bounded dynamic units. Ready runtime/host, runtime/supervision and runtime/registry (ELZ-013) stay unchanged. Author only the identity, bound and sleep policy. Ready state/lease (ELZ-028) decides which single owner may drive a durable Run; this one-node job does not use it, and the later cluster handle will.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- One node only; a cluster-wide directory is a separate, later handle.
- docs/architecture.md (OTP lifecycle and isolation) names DynamicSupervisor for assemblies that create bounded, named units. The admission either moves this handle to the assembly family or records the doc change; the worker does not decide it.

### RC-47

Slow local work away from the Unit, with cancel

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A Unit starts slow work that is not an Effect (a local computation, for example a bounded script run) under an injected supervisor and stays free for other inputs. The result goes to the registered name of the owner as one message, so after a restart it reaches the Unit that holds the identity. The caller supplies a wrap function that builds that message from (work_ref, outcome): this Piece owns no message form. The engine (RC-50) supplies the wrap that gives the info-message form of RC-45 with an RC-68 input inside. The async controller survives a receiver-only restart, retains its monitors and sends to the replacement; the replacement owner lacks volatile action knowledge. A result for cancelled or unknown work is late and never applied. Killed or timed-out work gives an explicit loss message. An Effect (a model call, a tool call, any outside write) is out of scope: delivery/effect-intent already runs it in a task away from its owner. Volatile: durable work stays with integration/jobs. Shared runtime boundary: RC-45 owns exactly one business info-message form: `{:durable_unit_input, input_id, input}`. `input_id` is a caller-stable UTF-8 binary of 1..160 bytes without NUL, scoped to one durable stream; `input` is caller-owned data. A direct sender constructs that tuple; no second intake wrapper or Envelope is introduced. RC-45 applies the same intake semantics to `step(server, input_id, input)` and this tuple. Sending the tuple is not an acknowledgement or evidence of commit; mailbox delivery is volatile. Only the synchronous step's applied/duplicate reply establishes that the input's result is committed. The caller owns resubmission with the same identity after an uncertain outcome, and identity collisions are errors inside the retained window. RC-46 routes that tuple unchanged to the current local Unit for the identity. It must not report a successful send as durable acceptance, and must explicitly refuse a stop/wake race it cannot resolve; no durable mailbox or exactly-once delivery is promised. RC-47 owns no durable-unit tuple: it calls the supplied `wrap.(work_ref, outcome)` and sends that returned term to the owner's registered name, resolved when sending rather than retaining an owner PID. RC-47 tests use a receiver process, not RC-45. RC-50 supplies the wrap that builds the RC-45 tuple with an RC-68 input inside; it supplies stable input identity and engine-read time. A result for an action no longer open is late and is never applied, including after durable-unit input deduplication has expired. On engine recovery RC-50 records a failed outcome with a loss class for an open volatile local-work action before admitting subsequent results; a later result is late. Neither RC-45 nor RC-47 implements this engine policy. An append returning `{:conflict, observed}` causes RC-45 to stop with `{:shutdown, :durable_unit_conflict}`. The ready Writer normally reloads and returns conflict; RC-45, not the Writer or RC-46, owns the stop policy. No losing candidate state or outbox is installed or offered. A pure transition may already have been evaluated before a result-append conflict; “applies nothing” means no committed losing result, not that no CPU work occurred. RC-46 uses this exact stop reason for the losing Unit on another node. Local Registry uniqueness is not cluster ownership.

**Acceptance:** Cancel during the work; result after cancel (late); worker crash (loss message with a reason class); deadline kill; owner restart while work runs (the result reaches the new owner by name, and the controller retains its work_ref and monitors while the replacement owner lacks volatile action knowledge: the owner decides, and the engine of RC-50 gives the open action a failed outcome with a loss class in its payload at start, so a result after that is late); a bound on concurrent work per owner with explicit refusal; no result payload in telemetry.

**Reuse:** OTP Task.Supervisor; runtime/registry (ELZ-013) for the stable name; delivery/time for the deadline inside one runtime. The Runner of delivery/aggregation is not reusable here: it starts a fixed branch set one time, has no cancel, and is read by a decision call, not by messages (pieces/delivery/aggregation/lib/elzoo/aggregation/runner.ex). Reuse only its loss classes killed, timed out and unavailable. Ready runtime/unit takes no info message. The first real receiver is the durable Unit of RC-45, joined in RC-50; this job tests against a test receiver process and has no RC-45 prerequisite.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- A wrapper over Task.Supervisor alone earns no row: the admission names the first caller that is not an Effect. Candidate: the local-work action of RC-50, first used for a Lua tool (RC-62).
- docs/architecture.md (OTP lifecycle and isolation) names Task for bounded asynchronous adapter work. Local work that is not adapter work needs a recorded decision at admission. The work here stays bounded and tracked: a work_ref, a deadline and a bound per owner.

### RC-48

Run lifecycle with several open waits, pause and continue

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A total lifecycle decision for a Run that can wait for more than one thing at once: a model result, tool results, an approval, a person, child Runs, an outside event, an event from a run resource; plus paused. A pure step over the phase, the set of open waits and one input of RC-68. Boundary: RC-12 owns Run and Attempt identity, the parent-child links and the Run outcome set; this Piece uses that set and owns only the phase, the open waits and pause. An input of an older Attempt leaves the phase unchanged. A cancel names one Attempt. Which Attempt is current and what a cancel names is the rule of RC-12: this Piece takes it and does not define it again. Owner decision 2026-11-12: time limits continue to count during a pause, and the pause has its own time limit. The person who continues a Run can give it a new time limit in the same recorded action. The total decision still names waits for child Runs and an outside event; no RC-68 version-1 action opens them, and tests supply those waits as data. Shared RC-12 / RC-48 boundary: `workflow/run` owns Run identity, Attempt identity/currentness, reciprocal parent-child links, approval persistence and the sole terminal Run outcome set: `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `out_of_budget`, `out_of_time`. A missing outcome means nonterminal; waiting and paused are phases, not outcomes. `completed` requires a committed completion record referencing durable evidence. `failed` may carry a reason such as retries exhausted or a refused required action; those reasons do not add Run outcomes. Action `unknown`, `late`, `expired`, `refused`, `resource_lost` and `retries_exhausted` remain action classifications, not alternate Run outcomes. In particular, an unknown external effect remains separately visible even if the Run later ends cancelled, failed or out of time; Run termination never asserts no effect occurred. A Run keeps one current execution Attempt for its execution operation. A retry keeps the Run and operation identity and records a distinct Attempt ID before execution. Concurrent actions belong to that execution Attempt; an effect-intent's numbered transport attempt is a different identity and must not be used as the Run's Attempt. Only the serialized durable Run owner can replace the current execution Attempt. Restoration alone neither creates a new Attempt nor retries an action. A result names its originating Attempt; an older or unknown Attempt does not change the Run phase or settle a current wait. A cancel names exactly one execution Attempt; a stale or unknown target is unchanged, not a Run-wide cancel. An accepted current cancel suppresses new work and records cancellation without claiming that an already reserved external send was prevented. An indeterminate cancellation write stops the owner; recovery reconciles that write before further work. Terminal outcomes are immutable; duplicate completion and late evidence cannot reopen the Run. Independent effect reconciliation may retain late evidence without rewriting the Run outcome. `workflow/bot-run-lifecycle` owns only phase, open waits, pause and continue. It uses `workflow/run`'s currentness classification and outcome validation rather than implementing either again. RC-50 invokes these boundaries and owns action dispatch/routing; it stores no second approval state or second restart protocol. RC-68/ELZ-114 owns action/input/outcome shapes, not Run outcomes. Approval requests and secret filling are engine work, never new pattern action kinds. Child-Run and outside-event waits remain accepted lifecycle data, but no v1 pattern action opens them. Wake has only recorded engine-read time and bypasses mid-run-message policy. A person's answer names one ask-person action and a host-verified person; no generic completed action outcome settles that ask, and text without an open ask is a new message.

**Acceptance:** Every (phase, input) pair has a defined result; a result for a closed wait is late (the RC-68 class); a cancel for an older Attempt changes nothing; pause stops new actions while results of open actions are still accepted; an empty budget gives paused or out of budget by declared policy; continue restores the open waits. Time limits expire during pause, including the pause's own limit; a continuation can record a replacement time limit in the same action. Child-Run and outside-event waits are supplied as data, not opened by v1 actions. Candidate Cure law: totality and late-input classification.

**Reuse:** Ready workflow/state-machine (the job-Run decision, pieces/workflow/state-machine/cure/elzoo/job_run.cure) is the model for one rule only: a result of a stale Attempt leaves the phase unchanged. Do not copy two of its points: its CancelRequested carries no Attempt and wins from every non-terminal phase, and its 'late success' means a current result after expiry. It stays unchanged under its ID; this is a new Piece ID. No statechart framework.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-49

Run resource: a live thing that a Run holds

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A Run can hold a live thing between actions: an OS program, a code sandbox, a browser, a call. Opening is an Effect with intent, call and outcome records. The resource belongs to one Run; Run identity is opaque text here. One action at a time uses it and the others wait in a bounded line. It can give events to the Run. It has a time limit and a cost per minute. The owner closes it when the Run ends with any outcome. A lost resource is reported to the Run as an input and is never reopened automatically. The resource event and 'Run ended' are input kinds of RC-68. Each resource has its own lease stream, apart from the log stream of the Run. Owner decision 2026-11-12: a nested callable of the same Run can use a resource only when the outer node gives its name in the typed input; a child Run cannot use a parent's resource. Each resource declares stay or close on pause; the default is stay until its own time limit. Its cost is recorded even when the budget is empty. Moving ownership to a different Run is a later form, not implemented or ruled out by this Contract.

**Acceptance:** Two concurrent actions on one resource are serialized; the line bound refuses explicitly; the Run-ended input of RC-68 closes the resource, whatever outcome it carries (the outcome value is opaque here; RC-12 owns the set); an orphan found after a restart is reported lost, and its close is an Effect that needs an effect-intent owner that the host started with a grant (with a read-only owner it is reported as closing, not closed); an open with unknown outcome is not retried automatically; time-limit expiry closes and reports; a resource event for a finished Run is late; minutes are counted in a workflow/budget unit. A same-Run nested callable can use only a resource named in its typed input; a child Run is refused. Exercise stay and close on pause, default stay through its own time limit, and retained cost with an empty budget. No ownership-transfer implementation in this version.

**Reuse:** delivery/effect-intent (ELZ-023) for the open and close records; the host decides activation one time, when the owner starts (the activate call in init, pieces/delivery/effect-intent/lib/elzoo/effect_intent.ex); an owner that starts without a grant stays read-only and answers read_only, and a fresh first start needs a grant too. state/lease (ELZ-028) for single ownership: a lease lives in the stream of the state that it guards, so a resource gets its own stream and does not contend with the log Writer of the Run. workflow/budget (ELZ-022) for the minutes. delivery/time for the limit inside one runtime. This Piece owns only ownership, serialization and close policy behind a provider behaviour, and ships with a test provider.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- No real provider in this job; the first provider (OS session, sandbox, browser or call) is admitted separately.

### RC-50

Run engine Assembly

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Run one pattern for one host (a Run or a room); the one durable Unit of a host comes from RC-46. The cycle: append the input (RC-45); give each RC-68 input to the pattern, a pure step of RC-68 (an engine record, such as a verdict of the host gate, is not an RC-68 input and goes only to the engine); check each action of the request for its payload schema (RC-44), an identity that is new in the Run, the host gate, budget, authority and its approval rule; perform each action by its kind (an Effect through delivery/effect-intent, local work through RC-47, a resource through RC-49); append each outcome as its own input with the action identity. The engine knows no pattern and decides nothing about the work. It does the fan-out, because core/operator gives zero or one output. Durable state: the engine keeps the pure Elzoo.Budget state as records in the durable Unit, and does not rely on Elzoo.Budget.Owner across a restart. The request charge: a Run has a cap on the outputs of its pattern step, and a Run without that cap does not start. The engine counts each output of the pattern step before it checks an action of it, whether or not the output is a valid request of workflow/pattern-step and whatever happens to its actions. The count never goes down within a Run, and it holds across a restart. The request charge is not the budget reserve of an action that the output proposes. An output is within the cap when the count that includes it is not above the cap: the cycle above and the five properties of the host gate apply to it unchanged, and each outcome of its actions is recorded, also after a later output went over the cap. An output over the cap gets nothing: the engine checks and performs no action of it, does not ask the gate for it, records no verdict, no outcome and no join for it, and makes no input for the pattern from it; the cycle above and properties four and five ask nothing for it, and a reader counts it as an output over the cap. After the first output over the cap the Run does not continue: by the exhausted-budget choice that workflow/bot-run-lifecycle takes (pause or finish) it pauses, or it ends with the Run outcome out_of_budget, which the engine commits through workflow/run, on the input that the admission fixes; an earlier rule of that Piece can end the Run first. An output of the pattern of a nested callable is counted too. For a room host the cap holds for each period, a room without that cap does not start, and an output over the cap of its period gets nothing, as above. It stores no approval state: RC-12 owns approval persistence and the release call at dispatch, and the engine calls RC-12. The engine sends the released writes of an action only through delivery/effect-intent. It records the full five-field intent identity, the canonical arguments and the binding reference of the action before it admits the intent; after a restart it uses those recorded values and never derives them again from current configuration, and it refuses an incompatible recovery. An :ok from RC-12 after a restart is not proof that nothing was sent: when the intent holds a reservation, the engine starts no new dispatch, follows the recovery of delivery/effect-intent and makes no replacement action. An intent that was admitted and has no attempt gets a defined outcome too, under the restart protocol that the admission of RC-12 fixes: the first dispatch when the approval, the host activation and the admission deadline all permit it, and a recorded not-sent outcome when the deadline passed or the schema check refused; a read-only owner stays the case named below. It appends each outcome payload that it takes from the result sink of an Adapter before it acknowledges; a crash after sink delivery and before that append is recorded as a lost-outcome input, and the action is never dispatched again. The host decides the activation of an effect-intent owner one time, when the owner starts. An owner that starts without a grant stays read-only for its whole life; the engine never grants, dispatches nothing through a read-only owner, reports it, and waits for a new owner that the host starts after reconciliation. Local work that was running at a restart gets a failed outcome from the engine at start, from its log, with a loss class in its payload (killed, timed out or unavailable, the words of delivery/aggregation), and is not started again automatically. An ask-a-person action carries a deadline: on each recorded input, a wake from the host included, the engine records the 'too old' outcome for each open ask that is past its deadline, with the recorded time of that input. The engine has no timer of its own. The engine classifies an unknown or a repeated outcome by RC-68 (late) before the join of a request and never gives a second outcome to the join. The acting principal is trusted text from the host, as domain/authorization takes it. Model output is a proposal. An unknown outcome is never retried automatically. The host gate: the host supplies one gate callable with an identity (id and version) when it constructs the engine; the callable and its identity are fixed for the life of the engine, its answer may depend on host state, and the identity does not identify that state. The engine asks the gate for each action, after the payload-schema and identity checks and before the budget reserve, the authority decision and an approval request; an action that fails its schema or identity check is never given to the gate. It gives the gate the checked action, the trusted context that the engine gives that action for authorization (the acting principal and the effect class of domain/authorization) and the tenant that the host gives for the Run. The engine uses that one host tenant as the tenant of the gate context, of the authorization proposal and of the intent identity, and never takes a tenant from an action payload. The gate gives back allow, or deny with a reason code. The gate decides only: it is local and synchronous, it performs no Effect, starts no work, holds no reservation and never changes the action, and a prompt return is a Need on the host because the engine has no timer. A raise, an exit or any other return value is a deny. This contract fixes five properties of the gate and leaves the mechanism to the admission. One: the gate call is outside the pure transition of the durable Unit and outside its offer callback, and a verdict is a committed record of the durable Unit before the engine acts on it; the engine acts on the committed verdict and never on a gate answer that is not committed. Two: each action has at most one effective verdict, the first one committed; the gate can be asked more than one time for an action before a verdict is committed, which is why the gate holds nothing, and it is not asked again after that.
