Three: a denied action gets exactly one action_outcome input of RC-68 with the outcome class refused and a payload of a declared schema that holds the reason code and the gate identity; it gets no budget reserve, no approval request and no intent, and the other actions of the request are not affected. Four: no stop of a process and no rejected or uncertain append leaves a request without a verdict, or a denied action without its outcome, once the Unit runs, or runs again, on a Store that accepts appends; a request with no action left after the schema and identity checks still completes. Five: a reader that uses only the read call of state/event-log can find, for each action given to the gate, the one verdict, and for each action of each request the one outcome that the engine applied, by the rules of the transition (a record counts only when its result is committed; the first verdict of an action; the first outcome that the accept of RC-68 answers applied, so not a late one and not one refused as incompatible; an action whose identity the Run already used is refused by the identity check, and its refusal never settles and is never counted for the earlier action with that identity), on a stream with no Store retention applied and skipping a staged tail that the read marks uncommitted. The absence of Store retention on a stream that is still to be read is a Need on the host. The gate never asks for an approval: an ask comes only from the policy of domain/authorization. An action that a nested callable proposes is checked by the same gate, with the trusted context that the engine gives that action for authorization. The gate has no withdraw call: a later change of host state does not reach an action that already has an allow verdict, and the gate adds no stop and changes none of the existing ones. The engine has no call after an outcome, and the outbox is the dispatch channel of the engine and is not a record.

**Acceptance:** With a test pattern and fake Adapters: a restart at each step boundary continues without repeating a settled action; a crash between result-sink delivery and the outcome append gives the lost-outcome input and no second dispatch; the budget cap holds across a restart; an approval that waits survives a restart; after a restart with a recorded release and a dispatched intent, RC-12 permits and still no second intent and no second send exist; after a restart the recorded intent identity, arguments and binding are used, and a changed Adapter or provider account in current configuration makes no second intent; an intent admitted with no attempt ends, after a restart, in one dispatch or in a recorded not-sent outcome, never in a stuck Run; with a read-only effect-intent owner no Effect is dispatched and the engine reports it; local work that was running at a restart gets a failed outcome with a loss class and a later result is late; an open ask past its deadline gets 'too old' at the next recorded input, and an answer after that is late; an action payload that fails its schema check is refused and the refusal is an input; an action identity that the Run already used is refused, and that refusal does not settle the earlier action with that identity, even while it is open; an action over budget or without authority is refused and the refusal is an input; an approval rule holds the action until a decision bound to the exact action; several actions of one request run concurrently and each outcome returns once; a late result of an old Attempt is ignored; a timeout after dispatch is recorded unknown and not retried; a Run without a budget does not start; a nested callable gets only its typed input and a budget share, where a share is a reserve on the parent budget plus a child budget identity whose cap is the share; Run end with each outcome closes every held resource (test provider); a local-work action is cancelled and its result is late; with a host gate that denies one action of a request, that action gets exactly one outcome refused as an input with a payload that holds the reason code and the gate identity, has no budget reserve, no approval request and no intent, and the other actions of the request run; a gate that raises, exits or returns another value is a deny; a gate that allows changes no other check; an action that fails its schema or identity check is never given to the gate, and a request with no action left after these checks still completes its join; the gate, when called, observes no budget reservation for the action in the Unit state, no approval request in workflow/run and no intent; a stop of the engine at each point between the gate answer and the settled refused outcome, and a rejection of the input append, a rejection of the result append and an uncertain append at each of those points, give exactly one refused outcome and no stuck Run once the Unit runs, or runs again, on a Store that accepts appends; when the gate is asked two times for one action and gives two different answers, only the first committed verdict has an effect; after a restart, an action with a committed verdict that waits for an approval or has an admitted intent is not given to the gate again; a reader that uses only the read call of state/event-log, on a stream with no Store retention applied, finds for each action the one verdict and the one outcome that the engine applied, and counts no late, repeated, incompatible or uncommitted record; a Run without a request cap does not start; with a gate that denies every action and a test pattern that answers each refused outcome with a new request of two actions: an output over the request cap gets no action checked or performed, no verdict, no outcome and no join, and no input for the pattern comes from it; under each exhausted-budget choice the Run reaches its stop on the input that the admission fixes (pause pauses, finish ends with the Run outcome out_of_budget); under each exhausted-budget choice, with a gate that allows one action of the last request within the cap and denies the others, and a fake Adapter that returns the result of that action only after the output over the cap, that outcome is recorded and the join of that request completes; a request whose actions are all allowed is counted, and so are an output of the pattern step that is not a valid request and a request whose actions all fail the schema or identity check; the count holds across a restart and never goes down; with a gate that allows each nested_callable action and denies every other action, and a test nested callable whose pattern answers each refusal with a new request, the child gives an output over the cap, and no action of an output over the cap is checked or performed, whether its outputs count against the cap of the Run or against a share of it; a room without a request cap does not start, and for a room host an output over the cap of one period gets nothing, as in the refusal scenario; the other scenarios run with a gate that allows every action. The Assembly has no model, tool or Lua dependency.

**Reuse:** A composition of ready pieces with the Pieces of this track. delivery/effect-intent: its API is advance, observe and status calls and it notifies nobody, so the engine observes; it keeps a digest, not the result; it has no network cancel; every recovery start is read-only unless the host callback grants activation at that start, a fresh first start needs a grant too, and a call to a read-only owner answers read_only (README; effect_intent.ex, init). workflow/budget: the owner process is volatile and a new owner starts at zero (README, restart section), so only the pure state crosses a restart; it has no share primitive. domain/authorization is a decision policy and takes a trusted proposer from the host; it is not a principal service. workflow/approval needs durable retention of its events to survive a restart: that retention is RC-12's. delivery/aggregation: use its join functions for the join of one request, not Aggregation.Runner. Only decision/2 is pure: accept/2 and mark_lost/3 read the clock function that the join map holds (aggregation.ex), and accept takes a core/envelope result (README); core/envelope (ELZ-003) comes through delivery/aggregation. So the engine reads the clock one time, records it in the input, binds a constant clock from that recorded time before each call, persists the join without its now function (the README gives the Map.delete form), and maps each RC-68 outcome class to an Envelope result or a loss kind by a declared table. A second, differing input for one branch makes the join conflicted, and an undeclared identity gives unknown_branch: the RC-68 classification comes first. It stores a digest, so result values come from the log. integration/ai-text gives content to a volatile result sink one time per attempt, with no replay. The host gate is a callable that the host supplies, not a new Piece and not a second authorization engine. The policy of domain/authorization has only exact typed matches and :any (README, Policy), so a range, pattern or comparison rule, or a switch that changes while the host runs, is a gate rule. workflow/run takes approval_request only with a ready Authorization ASK decision (README, command table) and workflow/approval runs decide again at release (README), so the gate cannot ask. The trusted authority context of domain/authorization has exactly proposer, effect_class, own and parent, and tenant is a field of the proposal (README, Schemas; authorization.ex), so the gate takes the tenant from the host and never from an action. The operator of runtime/durable-unit is pure and deterministic, and its inputs and state hold no funs (README, Needs); a transition returns a next state and zero or one output and cannot append an input (durable_unit.ex), and the offer callback must be prompt and nonblocking (README). So the gate call and each append that follows it are outside the transition and outside the callback (property one). The durable Unit keeps completed input identities only inside retention_count, and an evicted identity can apply again (README), so the transition itself must ignore a second verdict of an action (property two). A Store rejection before an input commits leaves the Unit usable and is not a restart, while a rejection of the result append and an uncertain Store answer stop the Unit, which is transient and is not restarted by its supervisor, and an uncertain append needs the Store confirmation before a reopen; a caller timeout is uncertain too and may leave the Unit running; an append conflict and a transition failure also stop the Unit (README; durable_unit.ex). So property four needs a rule for each of these and not only a rule for a restart. A request of workflow/pattern-step has no identity field (pattern_step.ex, decode) and an action identity has no length bound there, while an input identity of the durable Unit is 1 to 160 bytes (README), so the admission fixes a bounded derivation of the input identities of gate records. One mechanism that the ready pieces permit, not fixed here: the verdicts of one request as one input record of the durable Unit, and each refused outcome as its own input, both appended from outside the transition and read back from the Unit state. The cancel command of workflow/run makes the whole Run terminal (run.ex, perform cancel), workflow/run dispatches only in dispatch_approved, which needs an approval request from an ASK decision (run.ex), and delivery/effect-intent asks the host for activation only when an owner starts (effect_intent.ex, init). The gate adds no stop of its own to these. workflow/pattern-step v1 has the outcome class refused with an opaque payload (README), so a gate deny needs no new version. state/event-log has a read call (durable_log.ex, read/4); the pending call of state/outbox returns only pending items and its acknowledgement is open to any caller (outbox.ex), so a record reader uses the log and never the outbox. Nothing in runtime/durable-unit prunes its log (README), but every Store has a retain call (durable_log/store.ex) and a read below the retained point answers pruned, and a Store may show a staged uncommitted tail (the default of the Memory Store; the PostgreSQL Store reports atomic), so retention and the staged tail are Needs of a reader. A late or repeated outcome, an outcome that accept refuses as incompatible and an input with no committed result are committed input records too (durable_unit.ex; pattern_step.ex, accept), so a reader applies the rules of the transition (property five). The record encoding of the durable Unit is the fixed v1 form of its README ({1, {input_id, input}}, kinds input and result), so the admission fixes how a reader decodes the engine input inside it. runtime/telemetry events may be missing and are not an audit record or a usage meter (README), so a record of verdicts or usage never reads the telemetry. docs/capability-model.md already scopes the engine role as small and explicit, and the ELZ-025 study adopted none of the surveyed agent libraries as a host. workflow/pattern-step bounds the actions of one request (1..64, README) and not the number of requests, and a denied action reserves nothing (property three), so only a charge on the output itself bounds a pattern that answers each refusal with a new request. workflow/bot-run-lifecycle takes budget_exhausted and the budget rule (pause or finish) from its caller, and its README claims no proof of budget charging (README, operands and Needs), so the engine supplies that fact. That Piece supplies no timer and counts no requests: it checks the recorded deadlines on the inputs that it gets (README). After its terminal, Run-ended, Attempt, deadline and cancel checks, it applies the exhausted-budget rule to a pause or continue Control, to an outcome, person answer or resource event that it accepts for an open wait, and to every other input, which it classifies as work; an outcome that it does not accept for an open wait is late or unchanged, without that rule (bot_run_lifecycle.ex, classify and correlate; the Cure decide table). So after the first output over the cap there may be no further input to which that rule applies, and the admission fixes that input. workflow/budget seals one cap and one unit for each incarnation of an owner, and the Assembly chooses them again at the next start (README, D2 and D6); a reservation gives its estimate back at expiry and at release, and a reset sets committed and reserved to zero (README, D1, D4 and D5). So a count that is held in that Piece needs a rule that keeps it from going down. No new runtime, broker or job engine.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Every other wave-1 job of this track must be accepted first.
- Agent orchestration stays an Assembly, not a privileged kernel: the admission must name one non-agent caller (for example an approval flow) and show that the engine serves it unchanged.
- Not sized: the largest outcome payload (a model answer, a tool result) that the Store accepts as one record. The admission states that maximum and what the engine does above it. Agree it with RC-69 before either job is admitted.
- The restart protocol between workflow/approval and delivery/effect-intent is fixed at the admission of RC-12 (its rules (a) to (g)); this job follows it and does not define a second one.
- The admission fixes the mechanism of the host gate under the five properties of the contract: the callable, the fields of its trusted context, the reason code, the declared schema of the verdict record and of the refusal payload, the bounded derivation of the input identities of gate records, where the engine finds a request with no verdict and when it acknowledges the outbox item of a request, the rule after a rejected or uncertain append, an append conflict, a transition failure, an input_too_large answer or an input_id_conflict answer, including who reopens a Unit that stopped and, after an uncertain append, the Store confirmation before that reopen, how the refusal of a reused action identity names its request, because an RC-68 outcome carries only action_id, the byte bound of the reason code and of the gate identity so that a verdict record and a refusal payload always fit the record bound of the Unit, whether a verdict record carries a time that the engine read and starts the ask-deadline check, the declared form in which a reader decodes the records of the durable Unit, and which process asks the gate, with its supervision beside the Unit of RC-46. The gate stays a Need of the Assembly and gets no Piece in this job.
- Found in the review of the host gate and not decided here: workflow/run dispatches only through dispatch_approved, which needs an approval request, and it has no command for an Effect that authorization allows without an approval. The admission states how such an Effect has its intent recorded and admitted, and whether the engine checks the Run outcome before each such dispatch, local-work start or resource open, because the cancel command of workflow/run does not by itself stop them.
- A door for wave 4: an administration command of a caller with no pattern and no budget (the handle assembly/checked-command in docs/plans/bot-platform.md) needs the checks of one action in the same order, without the budget and with its own identity rule: payload schema, host gate, authority decision and approval rule. The admission records whether a later Assembly can reuse the check order of the contract without a Run of the engine, and in which form; RC-50 adds no API and no acceptance item for it. The engine is not expected to serve that command unchanged, because the engine refuses a reused action identity, needs a budget and records an outcome after the work; so that command need not be the non-agent caller that the admission names. If the admission shows that the engine serves it unchanged, it may name it, and this door closes.
- The admission fixes the form of the request charge under the contract: where the count is held and what keeps it from going down; the identity of one charge, because a request of workflow/pattern-step has no identity field; how a reader of the log tells an output over the cap from a request within it; the input on which the Run pauses or ends after the first output over the cap, and whether that needs an input of the host: for a last output within the cap that leaves no input to which the ready workflow/bot-run-lifecycle applies its exhausted-budget rule, the admission shows that input, or records that the stop waits for a wake of the host, or changes that Piece; whether the Run may stop when the count reaches the cap, before an output is over it, without taking any obligation from an output within the cap; whether the cap can change in the life of a Run; whether an output of the pattern of a nested callable counts against the cap of the Run or against a share of it, and in the second case how a share holds a request cap beside a cost cap, and which outcome the nested_callable action of the parent gets after the first output of the child over its share, so that the parent Run is never stuck; and, for a room host, the period of the cap and what the room does after the first output over the cap of a period.

### RC-51

Mark content that must not give orders

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Tool results other than named skill instructions from the pinned package manifest source, retrieved documents and messages from another member are content, not instructions. RC-51 does not mark those skill instructions; source kind comes from the registry snapshot, not a path or result shape. The Piece wraps content with its source, a trust class and a size bound, and keeps the mark through transformation and storage, so that a prompt builder and a guard can treat it apart from principal instructions. The mark travels inside the payload, under its own schema_id and schema_version, the key of core/port (ELZ-002); core/envelope is unchanged. Authority comes only from the principal; a mark never grants or removes authority.

**Acceptance:** The mark survives encode, decode and concatenation; unmarked input to a marked-only interface is refused; a marked value in a model request is delimited and labelled by a declared, tested format; nested marks keep the least trusted class. No claim that marking prevents prompt injection: that stays a stated assumption.

**Reuse:** core/envelope has no metadata extension and refuses unknown keys (pieces/core/envelope/README.md), so the mark is payload, not envelope metadata. integration/mcp-client marks discovery descriptors with trust 'untrusted' (discovery.ex) and returns tool results unmarked: this Piece applies the mark to results. workflow/context-selection keeps derived content as an untrusted proposal: use the same words. Author only the shared mark and the render rule.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-52

Tool registry

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Resolve a tool name that a model proposes to one real callable, from declared tool sources whose lists can change at run time (an MCP server). Resolution is a pure function over a snapshot of listings that the caller passes in. The snapshot identifies the resolved source kind so RC-56 can distinguish a pinned-package skill, a host listing tool and other tools without inferring kind from result shape or text. Finding a tool grants no permission to use it. The registry owns the model-facing alias: deterministic, inside the tool-name grammar of integration/ai-text ([a-zA-Z0-9_-], 1 to 64 bytes) and its bound of 64 tools, and mapped to (source, tool, version). An alias collision is refused, never a silent first match. A source keeps its own tool names. Version is the SHA-256 of the canonical descriptor (name, input schema, description, annotations), because an MCP descriptor has no version. A callable is an Adapter call or a nested callable by name (a tool written in Lua, RC-62). Resolution returns the callable with its argument schema and resolved source kind, or an explicit unknown, ambiguous or changed result.

**Acceptance:** Unknown name; resolution identifies the source kind from the snapshot, not result shape or text; the same tool name on two sources (two aliases, never a silent first match); an MCP name with a dot or over 64 bytes gets a valid alias; more than 64 tools is refused or cut by a declared rule; a descriptor whose digest differs in a fresh listing (changed, with the new version named); a tool absent from a fresh listing (removed); a removal after the last listing stays a dispatched unknown and is not claimed as detected; arguments get one validation result before dispatch; a later invalid_arguments refusal of mcp-client is the tool-call outcome and is recorded as a validator disagreement; an absent description is filled and a long one is cut by a declared rule. A descriptor from discovery with its trust field is accepted, a descriptor whose trust value is not the text untrusted is refused, and the trust field does not change the digest.

**Reuse:** integration/mcp-client gives inert, bounded discovery: a descriptor has five fields: trust (always the text untrusted), name, description, input_schema and annotations (discovery.ex, README); names follow [A-Za-z0-9_.-] up to 128 bytes (wire.ex); its snapshot is a local filter that can be stale. The registry accepts that five-field shape and refuses a descriptor whose trust value is not the text untrusted. The version digest of this job covers only the four content fields (name, input schema, description, annotations) and leaves out the constant trust stamp. The shared content-marking contract stays with RC-51. The argument check of mcp-client (ex_json_schema) runs too and can refuse invalid_arguments as a real outcome (binding.ex, README): when it refuses after the registry check passed, that refusal is the tool-call outcome and is recorded as a validator disagreement. A description can be absent there (discovery.ex), and ai-text needs a text description of at most 4,096 bytes (request.ex). integration/ai-text checks tool proposals with jsv and returns an undeclared or invalid call with arguments nil and a malformed tag (adapter.ex): that check decides for a tool declared in the request. RC-44 is expected to choose jsv too; if it does not, this job says which check decides. A disagreement is an explicit failure. The integration/agent-runner proposal (RC-17) asks for a stable host tool registry id and version and does not depend on this job today. No tool framework.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- RC-17 (integration/agent-runner) asks for its own host tool registry id and version. Before RC-17 is admitted, the coordinator makes it use this registry, so that two registries do not exist.

### RC-53

Token estimate before a model call

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Estimate the input size of a provider-neutral text request for a named model before the call, with a declared error direction: an upper bound, or a value labelled approximate. The value carries an estimator_id and an estimator_version, the fields that a window of workflow/context-selection names for its estimates. Used to refuse or shorten a request before spending. The reported usage of the provider stays the only usage fact. First provider: Anthropic.

**Acceptance:** Estimate against provider-reported usage on recorded Anthropic fixtures, with the observed error stated; an estimate below the reported value on a fixture fails the upper-bound claim; unknown model refused; tools and system text counted; an approximate value never presented as exact; no network call unless a provider count endpoint is the declared method.

**Reuse:** llm_db (pinned) for model limits; req_llm (pinned) and provider count endpoints where they exist. In workflow/context-selection the estimator_id and estimator_version sit on the window, and the estimate of a candidate is a bare number under that window. It refuses a byte estimate that is below the content bytes and does not check a token estimate: token accuracy and provider limits are external Needs there (README; context_selection.ex). No tokenizer is pinned: adopting one is a separate provider-prerequisite task (mix.exs, mix.lock, registry/providers.json).

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- The first deliverable is the provider-fit record: a local tokenizer or a provider count endpoint. The choice decides if this Piece is pure or an Adapter, so it comes before the Piece is admitted.
- A second provider (OpenAI) adds ELZ-087 as a prerequisite.

### RC-54

Prompt assembly and history compaction

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Build one provider-neutral integration/ai-text request from role instructions, named skill instructions from the pinned package manifest source, selected memory, history and a tool list in the ai-text tool shape (name, description, input_schema), within a token limit and within the hard request bounds of ai-text, with a declared order and a declared drop order. Named skill instructions rank with recipe role instructions and are built as instructions, never dropped. Untrusted content stays marked. An image or binary value appears only as a marked text reference to an Artifact. Compaction is a pure step that gives one model-call action (RC-68); the engine records the intent, the outcome and the original history. Keep-rule items (the goal, open actions, decisions, failed approaches) are structured records carried verbatim outside the summary; only free text is summarized. The summary is derived content with derived_from references.

**Acceptance:** Over-limit input drops in the declared order and never drops recipe role or named skill instructions or splits a tool call from its result; a tool result without its call is refused; the result respects 256 messages, 64 tools, 4096 bytes per tool description and the configured max_request_bytes of ai-text (at most 65,536 canonical bytes), and an input over them drops by the same order or is refused before admission; the request is deterministic for the same inputs; a compaction that loses a keep-rule item is found by set difference on item ids and refused; references to images keep only the last N by a declared rule; no image part is put in a request.

**Reuse:** Ready workflow/context-selection (versioned selection with an access check; derived content needs allow_derived) and the integration/ai-text request shape, which refuses image, document and parts content as unsupported_feature (pieces/integration/ai-text/lib/elzoo/ai_text/request.ex). The estimate of RC-53. Image input waits for a multimodal interface of a later wave. The join of the tool registry with the prompt is RC-56, not here. No prompt-template framework.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-55

Typed decision with bounded output repair

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A pattern step of RC-68 where a model gives one answer of a fixed set, or an answer that matches a schema. A non-matching output starts a bounded number of repair requests that carry a violation list. After the bound the result is an explicit failure, never a guessed value. integration/ai-text decides valid or invalid; the violation list comes from the RC-44 check of the same schema value over the returned content. If the two disagree, the result is an explicit failure.

**Acceptance:** Answer outside the set; valid after one repair; still invalid at the bound (explicit failure with the last violations); a refusal or empty output of the provider; an output cut by the length limit (finish length); ai-text and RC-44 disagree (explicit failure); each repair call visible to the budget of the caller; a fixed set with one element refused at construction; the step is deterministic for recorded inputs.

**Reuse:** integration/ai-text validates structured output with jsv and returns only {:invalid, :missing | :schema_mismatch | :invalid_json}, with no paths (adapter.ex); both ready providers also give the raw JSON text in content. Reuse it and RC-44. Author only the repair bound and the pattern step.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-56

Agent loop as a pure step

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** The agent loop as one pattern, not the base: a pure step of RC-68. Inputs: start, a model result, a tool result, a person's answer, a new message, cancel. Output: new state and zero or one request with a set of actions (call the model, call one or more tools, ask a person, run a nested callable, finish), all in the shapes of RC-68. The model proposes; the step maps each proposed tool call through the pure resolution of the tool registry (RC-52) over the snapshot in its state, and puts all calls of one model turn in one request. RC-65 captures the source set at Run start from the pinned recipe and then-read tenant settings; no model, tool or person adds sources, changed settings affect the next Run, and an explicit version-pin change does not change the captured set. Known-source tool lists may change by recorded input. This job owns the declaration result shape and accepts instructions or tools only when the resolved source kind in the registry snapshot permits them: pinned-package skill instructions and declarations, or host listing tool declarations, never inferred from result shape or text. A listing outside the captured set is refused without changing the snapshot. It owns named refusals for exceeding 64 tools and a total loaded-instruction byte bound; the worker proposes the latter at admission considering ai-text's 65,536 canonical-byte request cap. An instruction-overbound load adds no instruction; instructions are never cut and a repeat loaded skill adds nothing. A fresh listing reaches the step as the outcome of a tool-call action to a host listing tool: mcp-client discovery never sends tools/call, so a host tool wraps it and this job tests with a scripted one. It builds each model request with RC-54 and applies RC-69 to other tool results and skill files, never the SKILL.md body. The step has no clock, network or storage, and checks neither budget nor authority: the engine does that for every action.

**Acceptance:** Recorded-trace tests with no model and no network: a final answer; several tool calls in one turn (one request, each result returns once, in any order); an unknown tool (a result for the model, not a crash); a malformed proposal (each malformed tag of ai-text) reaches the model by a declared rule that request.ex accepts: it is echoed with an empty arguments object and its tag is the tool-result text, or the call is left out and the tag is plain content, and a proposal whose name is outside the tool-name grammar is always left out; a known-source listing changes the snapshot (a new tool usable in the next turn, a change over 64 tools refused); outside-set listing refused with snapshot unchanged; any other tool's exact declaration-shaped result adds neither instructions nor tools, while a pinned-package skill load adds instructions; an instruction-overbound load adds no instruction, instructions are not cut and a repeat loaded skill adds nothing; a proposal without an id cannot get a tool result and is answered by a declared rule; a tool that the engine refuses (the refusal returns as content, rendered by a declared rule that every supported provider accepts); a tool result after cancel (late); the turn limit (explicit outcome); the text 'done' from a model is not completion evidence.

**Reuse:** The ELZ-025 study surveyed agent libraries, Jido included, and adopted none because each owns the process host; they are references for the step table only. integration/ai-text returns an undeclared or invalid tool call with arguments nil and a malformed tag; a proposal id can be nil; a tool_result message needs a tool_use_id of 1 to 256 bytes (adapter.ex, request.ex). An assistant message that echoes a tool call needs an id of 1 to 256 bytes, a name in the tool-name grammar and an object for arguments, or the request is refused invalid_request (request.ex): a malformed proposal cannot be echoed as it came. integration/ai-text-openai (ELZ-087) refuses a request that holds a tool result with is_error true (open_ai.ex), so a refusal is not rendered as an error result there. Composes RC-52, RC-54, RC-69 and integration/ai-text. assembly/agent-product (RC-38) remains the later example Assembly and is not this Piece.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- The pure step is testable alone; its end-to-end example waits for RC-50.
- Not verified: that each provider accepts an echoed call to a tool that the request does not declare. The worker checks it on a recorded fixture before it chooses the echo rule.

### RC-57

Provider stream with tool calls (Anthropic first)

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Partial output of one model call, partial tool-call arguments included, as a bounded stream under the lifetime rules of delivery/data-stream: one acknowledged frame at a time. The start of a tool call (id and name) is carried in a chunk shape that this Piece owns; Elzoo.AiModel.StreamChunk is unchanged. The complete result is assembled once, within its own declared byte bound. A consumer that stops reading closes the local HTTP request; provider work and charges after the close are unknown. First provider: Anthropic. A second provider is a separate Piece of the same family, as with integration/ai-text.

**Acceptance:** Text only; a tool call split across chunks; a stream cut in the middle of a tool call (explicit incomplete, never a partial tool call dispatched); a slow consumer (one unacknowledged frame, then backpressure or close by declared policy); an assembled result over its byte bound (explicit refusal); a provider error mid-stream; the content, the tool calls (id, name, arguments) and the finish reason of the assembled result equal those of the paired recorded non-stream fixture; response_id, usage and provenance differ between two recordings and are not compared.

**Reuse:** Ready delivery/data-stream bounds one Anthropic text stream, keeps no decoded backlog, claims no remote cancellation and uses no ReqLLM (README). req_llm streaming is pinned (mix.lock): assess it against those bounds before adopting it. The ready tool_delta chunk carries only index and delta (pieces/integration/ai-model/lib/elzoo/ai_model/stream_chunk.ex). A new Piece ID, because it widens the ready Contract to tool calls.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- No other job of waves 1 to 3 needs this one; its first caller is the client stream of wave 4. Admit it when a worker is free, not before a job that blocks others.

### RC-58

Run inputs from people

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Two small pure policies over a Run, in the input and action shapes of RC-68. Wait for a person: a Run asks a named person one question, stops, and continues with the answer or a time-out; this is not an approval, which binds one exact action. The person's answer is its own RC-68 input kind: it names exactly one ask-a-person action and carries the host-verified identity of the person, never taken from message text. That action gets no other completed outcome. Text without an open ask is a new message. The time-out is the 'too old' outcome of the ask-a-person action, which the engine records with the time that it read: RC-68 has no time-out input kind and this job adds none. The policy has no timer. A durable timer belongs to the host and is not in this job: its wake is the RC-68 wake input, carrying no content, only engine-read time; it never goes through the mid-run-input policy. The engine (RC-50) records 'too old' for each open ask that is past its deadline. Mid-run input: a new message during an active Run follows one declared rule: queue, reject, interrupt, steer or side run.

**Acceptance:** An answer after the time-out is late; an answer from a person other than the named one is refused; two answers (the first stands); a person answer names its ask action and no other completed outcome settles that action; text without an open ask is a new message; wake bypasses every mid-run policy; each mid-run rule on the same recorded trace gives its declared result, and interrupt reports what was already delivered; a side run reads state and cannot change the first Run.

**Reuse:** Ready workflow/approval is the model for binding a person: it takes the verified identity as a Need. The open-wait, pause and continue rules are RC-48's. delivery/time is a volatile timer inside one runtime and is not used: a Run that sleeps cannot rely on it. Pure decisions; Cure where a law earns it.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### RC-59

Several MCP servers per tenant, from a build-time set

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Several MCP servers for one tenant behind the ready one-server Contract. Each server is one compiled Elzoo.McpClient.Binding module: server identity, endpoint and network policy are fixed at build. This Piece composes a declared, build-time set of bindings per tenant, each with its own credential Need and limits. Tool lists belong to server plus credential; RC-59 has one tenant credential per server and keys lists by tenant and server. Future per-person credentials require separate lists: MCP 2026-07-28 says lists MAY vary by authorization (https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools). It does not rename tools: the model-facing alias belongs to the tool registry (RC-52). One failing server does not block the others. A credential is a bearer token from an integration/secrets reference, supplied as the zero-arity credential Need of mcp-client; no OAuth. Streamable HTTP only. Tenant-added runtime servers need a new one-server runtime-endpoint Piece under integration/egress-policy in wave 5, bearer first; OAuth with integration/oauth-client is wave 9. These are out of scope here.

**Acceptance:** The same tool name on two servers stays distinct by server identity; lists for different tenants and servers remain separate; one server down (its tools are explicitly unavailable, the others work); a missing credential is refused credentials_unavailable and nothing is sent; a rejected or expired credential is refused authentication_failed after dispatch, and expiry is named only if integration/secrets reports it before the call; a tool-list change on one server is seen by listing again, with no push; per-server bounds; no credential in the results, errors or events that Elzoo emits.

**Reuse:** Ready integration/mcp-client over ex_mcp (pinned): the binding is compile-time (pieces/integration/mcp-client/lib/elzoo/mcp_client/binding.ex), configure takes no endpoint, there is no OAuth and no list_changed subscription, and a 401 or 403 gives authentication_failed. The consumer starts the application of ex_mcp, and its own telemetry stays the responsibility of the consumer (README). integration/secrets for credential references. A new Piece ID: the ready one-server Contract is unchanged.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- The stdio transport is out of scope until a session-capable OS provider exists.

### RC-60

Lua runtime fit and bounded sandbox

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Run Lua source with hard limits on steps, memory, call depth and value size, and with no file, network, clock, random or process access unless a function is given explicitly. Two uses: load a recipe file one time and return plain data; run a small pure function during a Run, where the same input gives the same output. Inside a Run only a limit that the runtime counts deterministically (steps, call depth, value size, and memory only if it is counted for each allocation inside the runtime) decides a result; each of a wall-time stop and a memory stop that depends on garbage-collection timing is a host outcome that is recorded as an input and never computed again on replay. The capability policy is an allow-list with a version identity: by any path (a global, the environment table, a function or a table of functions that a listed function returns, a method or a metatable of a value), a source reaches the runtime function behind a name only if the policy lists that name; a use of an unlisted name ends in the outcome that the admission fixes and never reaches a runtime function. A function that a listed function returns without a name of its own, such as the iterator of ipairs, is covered by the listing of that function. The policy lists the only library names that a source can use. Owner decision (option A), 2026-11-14: ELZ-163 policy version 1 is helper-free and refuses every helper name as unlisted_name, an exception to the every-policy-version helper requirement; runtime/lua-stdlib adds the safe JSON/text/time helper set as the next policy version. RC-62 and RC-66 depend on lua-stdlib and never use helper-free version 1. The policy, not the standard library of the chosen runtime, is the contract toward the author of a source. A source cannot catch a limit stop: a step, memory, call-depth, value-size or wall-time stop ends the evaluation.

**Acceptance:** Provider-fit probe first: an infinite loop, deep recursion, a large allocation and a string bomb end in a named refusal within the bound; each escape path of the library surface of the candidate runtime (load, require, os, io, debug, metatables on shared values) never reaches a runtime function and ends in the outcome that the admission fixes for an unlisted name; the host process survives each case. The same pure function and input give the same output across runs and restarts when a limit that the runtime counts deterministically decides. Returned data is plain: no function or reference leaks. Errors carry no host paths. Against the allow-list: for each candidate the probe records the names that a source reaches by each path of the contract, found from inside the sandbox, from the host side through the interface of the runtime (the environment table and each shared metatable) and by probing a fixed list of standard-library names through values, and each difference from the policy; the chosen runtime reaches exactly the policy on all three; for each catching function that the runtime supplies (pcall, xpcall, coroutine.resume, coroutine.wrap), the probe shows that it is not reachable or that a source that uses it does not continue after a step, memory, call-depth, value-size or wall-time stop.

**Reuse:** Owner decision, 2026-11-14: use reference PUC Lua 5.4.9 outside the VM over a Port with a small C host, following the completed ELZ-159 fit record. ELZ-160 is the separate provider prerequisite: mix.exs, mix.lock, registry/providers.json, the dependency register and a narrow runtime adoption check with full local and hosted checks. The official mirror commit is accepted for its byte-identical library sources; the absent luac.c is not needed. No Lua provider is adopted by this publication. runtime/os-isolation is not security isolation (its README). Author only the limit and capability policy in the later Piece task, using ELZ-159's policy answers as admission input; those answers are not finalized by ELZ-160. A fork follows docs/development/08-upstream-and-forks.md and is not assumed. The alternative in-VM path still requires the explicit exception decision and named integration test of docs/architecture.md (OTP lifecycle and isolation); the owner selected the outside-VM path.

**Blockers:**

- Both portions are published and merged: ELZ-163 sandbox and ELZ-174 helper set; the helper set is part of runtime/lua-sandbox.

### RC-61

Recipe data: callables, package and tenant settings

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** The plain data that a recipe gives: named callables (a graph of nodes, an agent, a typed decision), each with a typed input and output; nodes that state what they need and give; links that add only order and branches; outcome links, each for one outcome class of RC-68; rules such as an approval on an action; bindings apart from the graphs. A rule can only add a requirement: it never relaxes the authorization policy of the consumer. Callables nest by name with a depth limit. A loop is a node that re-runs a nested callable (RC-63), never a back-edge, so a graph has no legal cycle. A package manifest gives name, version, needs, permissions and each skill that the recipe uses, with its version and tools; the public Agent Skills format has no tools field. Package checking refuses malformed skills before load, following RC-67. Tenant settings are checked against the schema that the package declares; a secret setting is a reference.

**Acceptance:** Structural validation: unknown node kind, dangling name, a need with no giver, a decision node with a missing branch, any cycle, depth over the limit, a link to a missing outcome; malformed skill refused at package checking; each skill's version and tools declared in the package manifest; encode and decode round-trip is stable; a setting against the schema (wrong type, missing, extra, a secret given as a value is refused); version identity is explicit.

**Reuse:** Ready workflow/dependency-graph: validate on the needs graph finds cycles and unknown dependencies within its bounds (1,000 nodes, 10,000 edges). It has no node kinds, depth limit or data types: those are written here. Readiness is a run-time question and is not in this job. Rules use the policy identity of domain/authorization and the binding of workflow/approval; a recipe defines no second rule format. RC-44 for payload types. Data and validation only; nothing executes here.

**Blockers:**

- All three RC-61 portions are published: ELZ-161 recipe-graph, ELZ-162 recipe-config and ELZ-164 recipe-package. No output remains pending admission in RC-61.
- Owner decisions, 2026-11-14: callable kinds graph, agent and typed decision; node kinds call, tool, ask, decision, map and loop. Map and loop are bounded re-runs of a named callable, never back-edges. The handle remains domain/recipe-graph. The independent graph and config tasks are published as ELZ-161 and ELZ-162.
- Coordinator decision, 2026-11-14: ELZ-164 accepts decoded skill frontmatter plus Markdown body; raw YAML/SKILL.md syntax and folder provenance belong to the later reader (RC-67). Package identity digest, opaque versions, declarative needs/permissions, frontmatter fields and fixed bounds are admitted in the task.

### RC-62

Module functions for Lua, and tools written in Lua

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Expose the declared functions of a module to Lua from a function manifest that this job defines: name, version, argument and result schema (RC-44), cost in a workflow/budget unit, and permissions. Bindings resolve against a list of manifests that the caller supplies; there is no run-time discovery. Arguments are checked before the call and results before return. At load time a binding call builds data and performs no Effect. A tool written in Lua is a pure sandboxed function with a schema. The registry (RC-52) resolves its name to a nested callable, and it runs through the nested-callable action of RC-68, not as one Effect: only a pattern step gives a request. workflow/lua-tool is that step. It asks for one local-work action that runs the function in the sandbox from the start, with the recorded module results replayed in call order. That run ends in a completed outcome whose payload is the final result or the first module call that has no recorded result. Each module function of the manifest list is an entry of the tool registry (RC-52) with an Adapter binding that the caller supplies. For a module call the step asks for one tool-call action by the registry alias of that function, keeps its recorded result, and asks for the run again. Sandbox state is never persisted.

**Acceptance:** A wrong argument type or count is refused with the function and field named; a binding for a missing or wrong-version module is refused at load; no Effect happens at load time (a recording Adapter stays empty); a Lua tool that loops is cut by the sandbox bound and reports a tool failure; a Lua tool cannot reach a module that its recipe did not declare; a Lua tool with two module calls gives its two module-call actions one at a time, in order, each after a sandbox run, and the same final result after a restart between them; a replay whose call order differs from the record is an explicit failure.

**Reuse:** RC-60, RC-44, RC-52 and the package declarations of RC-61; workflow/budget (ELZ-022) for the cost unit. The engine performs the sandbox run as local work through RC-47; this job needs only the RC-68 shape of that action and tests the step on recorded traces. runtime/faces (RC-04) is the operations face (config, control, readiness) and has no function manifest: it is not a prerequisite. docs/capability-model.md keeps run-time discovery out of the kernel. No code-generation framework is assumed.

**Blockers:**

- All RC-62 portions are published: ELZ-177 (module calls in runtime/lua-sandbox, in place), ELZ-178 runtime/lua-binding and ELZ-179 workflow/lua-tool.
- Coordinator decisions, 2026-11-14: the sandbox answers a binding call itself (data at load time, a replay of recorded results at run time), so there is no callback into the caller; a refused argument is located by the function name and the structural path of core/payload-schema, which hides member names, and no second validator is written; the ready engine has one binding for each action kind, so a Run that mixes patterns needs the engine to choose a binding by the schema of the action (a later task), and a nested child is wrapped by the host so that its finish has the form of its parent.

### RC-63

Graph pattern with node outcomes

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** The graph pattern as a pure step of RC-68: from the recipe graph, the recorded results and one input, decide which nodes are ready and give one request with their actions. Needs and gives set the data order; links add order and branches; map runs one node per item with a maximum count, a concurrency bound and a part-failure rule, and map items are not graph nodes; loop is a bounded re-run of a nested callable, never a back-edge. Each node outcome class of RC-68 follows its outcome link, or the Run fails. A node with a recorded result does not run again. No automatic retry after an unknown outcome.

**Acceptance:** Recorded-trace tests: a graph with needs and gives only; a decision in the middle selects a branch; a join after a decision becomes ready when the taken branch completes, and the branch that was not taken is skipped, not waited for; map over N items with a bound and one failed item under each part-failure rule; loop at its maximum; each outcome link; a missing outcome link fails the Run explicitly; replay after restart does not run a settled node again; results in any arrival order give the same decision; a nested graph gets only its typed input.

**Reuse:** Ready workflow/dependency-graph: validate and readiness give the AND-join of acyclic needs only. Map a completed node to its completed lifecycle, never to a fact (a model-declared fact never satisfies). A cancelled or closed target never satisfies, there is no join policy beyond 'every blocking prerequisite satisfied', and every blocking cycle is an issue: thus either-of joins after a decision, skipped branches and loops are written here. Its bounds are 1,000 nodes and 10,000 edges. readiness takes a now value: the step passes the recorded time of its input. Ready delivery/aggregation: decision/2 is pure and gives the completion status of a map (all, partial, failed). accept/2 and mark_lost/3 read the clock function that the join map holds (aggregation.ex), and accept takes a core/envelope result (README); core/envelope (ELZ-003) comes through delivery/aggregation. So the step keeps the join without its now function, binds a constant clock from the recorded time of its input before each call, and maps each RC-68 outcome class to an Envelope result or a loss kind by a declared table. It stores a digest, not the payload, so map result values come from the recorded results. delivery/retry-timeout gives the retry words. Candidate Cure laws: no node runs twice, and every declared item appears in the map result.

**Blockers:**

- Both RC-63 portions are published: ELZ-166 node-outcome and ELZ-168 graph-run. No output remains pending admission in RC-63.
- Coordinator decision, 2026-11-14: the recipe data of ELZ-161 holds no concurrency bound and no part-failure rule for a map, so the caller of graph-run supplies them; the collection of a map is the need named items and a loop continues while its callable says so, up to max_iterations. graph-run writes its own join, skip and map-count rules over recorded outcomes and calls neither workflow/dependency-graph nor delivery/aggregation.

### RC-64

Recipe check before a run, and for a graph that a model gives

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Check a recipe before it can run: structure, the needs of each node, permissions, budgets, an upper cost limit from node budgets and map and loop maxima, and tool sources that exist. A pure checker. The same check accepts a graph that a model gives at run time as plain data, carried by the nested-callable action of RC-68, with added limits: only the callables and tool sources of its agent node, no new authority, a budget share, a node and depth maximum, data only. A refused graph returns its reasons as content for the model.

**Acceptance:** Each structural fault of RC-61 refused with a located reason; a permission that the recipe does not declare; a budget over a tenant cap that the caller supplies; an unbounded map; a model graph that names a callable outside the sources of its agent, asks for more budget, exceeds the node or depth maximum, or carries code; the same input gives the same verdict.

**Reuse:** The validation of RC-61; the registry snapshot of RC-52 for tool sources; ready workflow/budget for the units, with one cap per budget identity, which is not a tenant quota (a quota is RC-08); its critical_path_waves is a pure worst-case time check that assumes wave barriers and has no identity and no cap; domain/authorization for permissions. One checker for load time and run time. That a recorded model graph is read again after a restart, and the model is not asked again, belongs to the engine and is tested in RC-66.

**Blockers:**

- Both RC-64 portions are published: ELZ-167 recipe-check and ELZ-169 runtime-graph. No output remains pending admission in RC-64.
- Coordinator decision, 2026-11-14: the recipe data of RC-61 holds no cost, tool requirement or tenant grant, so the caller supplies them as facts; needs and permissions are compared as opaque strings with no call of domain/authorization; a structural fault is located at its part. For a model graph the host supplies the callables and tool sources of the agent, the depth and the budget share.

### RC-65

Recipe versions and the pin of a running Run

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Keep immutable recipe versions per tenant with publish and go-back; only a checked version can be published. state/recipe-store holds the versions, the published pointer, the check gate and the pin records, and owns the one rule that needs them together: a version that a live Run pins is not deleted. state/artifact has no cross-stream transaction, so the pin records, the published pointer and a delete mark live in one stream per tenant that recipe-store owns, under head-revision CAS: a pin and a delete are put in one order there, and the Artifact delete happens only after its delete mark is committed. Recipe versions are written with access expiry and retention set to never, and only recipe-store calls delete or reap on them. workflow/version-pin is the pure rule of which version a Run uses: the version at its start, changed only by an explicit input. At startup version-pin captures the Run source set from that pinned recipe and the then-read tenant settings. No model, tool or person adds sources; settings changes affect the next Run, and even an explicit version-pin change does not expand or change the captured source set. Publishing a new version changes no running Run.

**Acceptance:** Publish of an unchecked version refused; go-back selects an older immutable version; two concurrent publishes give a defined order; a Run started before a publish finishes on its own version; settings changes affect the next Run, not the captured source set, and an explicit version-pin change leaves that set unchanged; deleting a version that a live Run pins is refused; a pin and a delete of one version at the same time give one defined winner; reap does not remove a recipe version; tenant A cannot read the versions of tenant B.

**Reuse:** Ready state/artifact (ELZ-096) is already tenant-owned and versioned, with tenant-scoped streams and head-revision checks on concurrent appends: keep the recipe bytes and immutable versions there. Its head is the highest committed non-deleted version, so go-back needs the separate published pointer that this job writes. Artifact says 'No cross-stream transaction', its delete is a public call, and reap deletes a version past access_expires_at_unix_ms or retain_until_unix_ms; both fields accept :never (README). state/event-log (ELZ-009) gives the CAS stream of recipe-store. A consumer that deletes in the Artifact scope of recipes directly breaks the pin rule, and the Contract says so. integration/sql is Store-only and gives no general SQL API. The check of RC-64.

**Blockers:**

- Both RC-65 portions are published: ELZ-170 version-pin and ELZ-171 recipe-store. No output remains pending admission in RC-65.
- A platform release that changes pattern state is not covered here; that belongs to RC-01.

### RC-66

Run a recipe against scripted models and tools

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Run a recipe end to end through the real engine and patterns with scripted model and tool Adapters, no network and a controlled clock. The harness takes only Adapter modules from an allow-list that the caller supplies; any other Adapter is refused at start. The result is the full record of steps. This harness is how a recipe author and a Piece worker test without a provider.

**Acceptance:** A scripted model that gives tool calls, a wrong-schema answer and a final answer; a tool that fails, times out (unknown) and is refused; a Lua tool with a module call; a graph that the scripted model gives is recorded, and after a restart it is read again and the model is not asked again; the run is deterministic and repeatable; an unscripted call fails the test explicitly; an Adapter outside the allow-list is refused at start.

**Reuse:** The engine (RC-50), the patterns (RC-55, RC-56, RC-63), the registry (RC-52), the check (RC-64) and the Lua layer (RC-60, RC-62); ExUnit. The fakes of the ready Pieces are .exs test helpers (for example test/support/ai_text_test_helper.exs, which the piece.json of integration/ai-text lists); mix elzoo.take copies them as tests and they are not compiled modules. integration/ai-text also ships the scenario kit Elzoo.AiText.Conformance in lib. This harness ships compiled scripted Adapters, modelled on both.

**Blockers:**

- Both RC-66 portions are published: ELZ-181 and ELZ-183 (in place on runtime/recipe-test). The harness ships the glue that turns the finish of a nested child into the form of its parent, and it needs the engine changes ELZ-172 and ELZ-180.

### RC-67

Skill: instructions and files loaded on demand

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A skill follows the public Agent Skills format (https://agentskills.io/specification, accessed 2026-11-12; no spec version): a folder with SKILL.md, YAML frontmatter and Markdown instructions. Required name is 1–64 lowercase alphanumeric or hyphen characters, has no leading, trailing or consecutive hyphens, and matches the folder; required description is 1–1024 characters. Malformed skills are refused at package checking, not load. V1 skills come only from the recipe package; MCP io.modelcontextprotocol/skills is out of scope, and future use requires person approval of the exact digest. A model sees only descriptions until it asks for one. A skill is a registry tool source (RC-52); asking is a recorded tool call whose result holds instructions and the tools declared by the pinned package manifest, using RC-56's declaration shape. Trust comes from the pinned package manifest source, never the path or result shape. Named instructions rank with recipe role instructions, RC-54 builds them as instructions and never drops them, and RC-51 does not mark them; other tool results remain marked content. RC-56 accepts them only for the resolved pinned-package skill source kind, owns the 64-tool and total loaded-instruction byte bounds and named refusals, refuses an instruction-overbound load without adding instructions, and adds nothing for a repeat loaded skill. A load whose tools would pass 64 is refused whole with RC-56's named refusal: it adds neither instructions nor tools. The package manifest names which tools a skill brings; the host gives their descriptors as a fact, as for every other tool of the Run; the tools of a loaded skill are listed in a source of their own, of kind other, so their results are never declarations. An agent start may name one skill: its load is the same recorded tool call, made before the first model call. Instructions are never cut; RC-69 may cut skill files, not the SKILL.md body. A skill grants no authority, including through allowed-tools frontmatter; tools still pass engine checks. Scripts are stored and read but never executed in v1. RC-61's package manifest names each skill with its version and tools; the pinned recipe fixes skill versions.

**Acceptance:** Malformed folder or SKILL.md frontmatter refused at package checking, including name and description limits; unknown skill; a skill whose declared tool is not permitted for the principal (the instructions load, the tool is refused at use); a skill file over the bound of RC-69 is cut to a reference but the SKILL.md body is never cut; a load that would pass 64 tools is refused whole with the named refusal of RC-56 and changes neither instructions nor tools; the declared tools of a loaded skill are listed in the next model request and not before; a declared tool with no descriptor is refused at the start; an agent start that names a skill loads it before the first model call; an instruction-overbound load adds no instruction and repeat load adds nothing; allowed-tools grants no authority; scripts are stored and read, never executed; a changed skill version does not change a running Run; with the harness of RC-66, a load survives a restart and is not done again.

**Reuse:** Public Agent Skills specification, https://agentskills.io/specification, accessed 2026-11-12 (no spec version); state/artifact for files; RC-52, RC-54 and RC-56; the package manifest of RC-61; the cut rule of RC-69; the pin of RC-65; the harness of RC-66.

**Blockers:**

- RC-67 is published as ELZ-182. Coordinator decisions, 2026-11-14: the YAML frontmatter is read with the adopted library (ELZ-176) behind a scan that refuses aliases, merge keys, document markers, duplicate top-level keys and tabs; ELZ-182 listed no tool. Coordinator decisions, 2026-11-15 (the owner asked to close the gap in the code, not in the plan text): ELZ-235 lists the declared tools of a loaded skill from descriptors that the host gives, with no change of workflow/agent-loop, whose declare step already refuses a load whole when its listing is refused; ELZ-236 lets an agent start name one skill; ELZ-237 shows both with the harness of RC-66, the restart included; the Piece stores nothing, the caller holds the bytes of the files.

### RC-68

Pattern step protocol: inputs, one request with actions, outcomes

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** The one shared shape between a pattern and the engine, as data and pure validation. A pattern step takes state and one input and gives new state and zero or one request: the core/operator shape. A request holds a bounded set of actions. Each action has an identity, a kind, and a payload keyed by the (schema_id, schema_version) of core/port. Validation here checks that an identity is unique in its request; the engine checks that it is unique in the Run, against its log (RC-50). Each outcome is an input that names exactly one action identity. Each input carries the time that the engine read when it recorded the input: a pattern gets time only from there. Owner decision 2026-11-12: three closed, versioned sets. Action kinds v1: model call, tool call, local work, ask a person, run a nested callable (by name, or as data that passed the recipe check), open a run resource, use a run resource, close a run resource, finish. The three resource actions are distinct. Input kinds v1: start, action outcome, a person's answer, a new message, a run-resource event, cancel of one Attempt, Run ended, wake. Wake has no content, only engine-read time, and never goes through the mid-run-input policy. A person's answer names exactly one ask-a-person action and carries the verified identity of the person; that action gets no other completed outcome. Text without an open ask is a new message. Outcome classes v1 remain completed, failed, refused, late, unknown, out of budget, tries used up, too old, resource lost. New versions only add members to any of the three sets, never change or remove them, and older records stay readable. Excluded from v1: start a child Run, wait for an outside event, a part of an answer to a channel, a message to another member, make or remove a binding, give an inside event, give a run resource to a person. These enter in later versions with the wave that performs them. Ask for an approval and fill a secret are never action kinds: the engine does them. This Piece performs nothing and knows no pattern.

**Acceptance:** Construction and validation of each kind; a request with two actions of one identity refused; a request over the declared action bound refused; an outcome that names an unknown action is classified late and not applied; a second outcome for one action is classified late; an unknown kind or version refused, not guessed; encode and decode round-trip stable; no payload content in errors. The Run outcome set is not defined here: RC-12 owns it. Candidate Cure law: each action of a request gets at most one applied outcome.

**Reuse:** Ready core/operator (state and one input give new state and at most one output) is the step shape and stays unchanged: the request is its one output. core/port gives the payload key. delivery/effect-intent (ELZ-023) and delivery/retry-timeout (ELZ-006) already have words for dispatched, unknown and exhausted retries (the :exhausted decision and the retries_exhausted dead-letter reason of retry-timeout). The job records a table that maps each outcome class to the existing term where one exists; 'tries used up' and 'too old' are draft names for that table, not a second vocabulary. No engine code and no pattern code here.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- Admit this job first. RC-48, RC-49, RC-50, RC-54, RC-55, RC-56, RC-58, RC-61, RC-62, RC-63, RC-64, RC-69 and RC-70 use these shapes and must not define their own.

### RC-69

Bounded tool results

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** A large tool result is cut to a declared bound, or kept whole as an Artifact with a reference and a bounded preview. Skill files may be cut, but the SKILL.md instruction body is never cut; RC-56 refuses instruction loads over its total bound instead. The cut decision is pure. Storing is an outside write, so it is not local work (RC-47): it is a tool-call action of RC-68 to a host tool that wraps the put of state/artifact behind an effect-intent Adapter. The arguments of an intent hold at most 65,536 bytes, so they name the recorded outcome (its record reference and its digest) and never hold the result: the Adapter reads the bytes from the record and checks the digest before the put. The model never sees that tool. The engine performs it, with the tenant, principal, scopes, access terms and retention of the caller. The result stays marked as untrusted content (RC-51).

**Acceptance:** A result under the bound passes unchanged; a result over the bound becomes a reference plus a bounded preview, and the cut is visible in the value; a skill file may be cut but the SKILL.md body is never cut; a binary result is handled by reference only; a store that is refused or unknown, or a result over max_bytes of the Artifact store, leaves the bounded preview and a named note, never the full result; the mark survives the cut.

**Reuse:** state/artifact (ELZ-096): put is a direct call of the Piece and no effect-intent Adapter exists for it, so this job writes that Adapter. delivery/effect-intent (ELZ-023) refuses arguments over 65,536 bytes (effect_intent.ex), far below the max_bytes of an Artifact, so the Adapter takes a reference and reads the bytes from state/event-log (ELZ-009); a put with an unknown outcome leaves uncertain staging. put needs the tenant, principal, scopes, access terms and retention of the caller, and one Artifact holds at most the configured max_bytes (pieces/state/artifact/README.md). RC-51 for the mark. Author only the bound and the cut rule.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.
- The full result is in the record before the cut, because each input is recorded first. If the admission of RC-50 sets a largest outcome payload below a tool result, the cut moves to the engine side as a rule that the caller declares. Agree it with RC-50 before either job is admitted.

### RC-70

Stall verdict from the record

**Product aims:** P13. Bot platform track; see docs/plans/bot-platform.md.

**Contract:** Decide from the recorded inputs and requests of a Run (RC-68 shapes) that it makes no progress, apart from retry policy. A pure function over a bounded window of the record. It gives a verdict with a reason class and stops nothing: the caller decides.

**Acceptance:** On recorded traces: repeated identical actions over a declared count; no new input within a declared bound, with the time of each input read from the record and the current time given as a function argument, not as a new input kind; a healthy long wait for a person or an approval that is not a stall; a window over the bound is refused; the verdict is deterministic for the same record.

**Reuse:** The orchestration reference study names the stall idea. delivery/retry-timeout owns retry exhaustion, which is a different fact. No timer and no process here.

**Blockers:**

- Not published; coordinator must admit exact API, Needs, failure cases, evidence and ownership before assigning an ELZ ID.

### ELZ-116

ELZ-116 — Run and Attempt lifecycle with durable approvals

Publication copy: [tasks/ELZ-116-run.md](../../tasks/ELZ-116-run.md).

**Product aims:** P01, P02, P03, P05, P06, P07, P08, P11, P13.

**Acceptance:** Restart, late child result, duplicate completion, unknown spawn/cancel outcome, parent wakeup loss, stale Attempt and restored approval bypass. Child death observed; completion requires durable evidence. Approval restart windows, as composition tests with a recording Store, a valid current context, host activation and a fake Adapter that counts sends: an approval that has no evidence yet is an open wait after a restart; a crash before the release event is durable runs release/4 again with the stored fresh evidence, for scope :once, :run and :root each, and evidence that is expired or too old by then gives a refusal; a crash after the release event is durable and before the intent is admitted runs revalidate/3 and starts a dispatch only on :ok, with one intent and one send at the end; a crash after the intent is admitted and before a dispatch ends in one dispatch or in a defined not-sent outcome, never in a stuck Run; a crash after the dispatch and before its outcome is recorded gives no second intent and no second send, also when revalidate/3 answers :ok, and the outcome is reconciled or stays unknown; in each run the Store holds the release event before the first record of the intent. With a recorded release, each of these gives a refusal and no new dispatch: the policy identity changed (:policy_changed); the current decision, action or escrow changed (:requirement_changed, :payload_changed, deny_at_release); the authority of the approver no longer covers the escrowed writes (:approver_not_authorized); the evidence is expired (:expired), too old (:evidence_too_old) or revoked (:revoked). These named reasons are the cases; 'each change of authority' is not a case. :already_released from release/4 starts no dispatch. This job makes no replacement action for an action whose outcome is unknown.

### ELZ-117

ELZ-117 — Durable Unit

Publication copy: [tasks/ELZ-117-durable-unit.md](../../tasks/ELZ-117-durable-unit.md).

**Product aims:** P13.

**Acceptance:** Crash before the input append (the sender gets no acknowledgement); after the input append and before the result append (the transition runs again and gives the same result); after the result append and before the outbox offer; during a checkpoint write. Replay gives the same state as the uninterrupted run for the listed scenarios. A duplicate input inside the retention bound is not applied twice; a test shows the stated limit past the bound. A Writer conflict stops the Unit with the named error and applies nothing. A checkpoint with another fold_version is not used and recovery replays the log. Recovery fails closed when the needed log prefix was pruned. A record with an unknown schema_version is refused. A transition that fails on a recorded input gives a declared stop, not a restart loop. Ready runtime/unit keeps its Contract unchanged.
